# 22. Mindset and planning

## Define your reason, your meaning of success and your relationship to money

### Your core reason for building the business

Knowing your personal reason keeps you going through the slow early phase, so write it down and revisit it. The reason is private and does not need to satisfy anyone else. [L1039.001]

A personal reason strong enough to sustain effort over months is required. Without it, people tend to give up when facing rejection, customer service problems, or technical hurdles. [L0826.005]

**Procedure: finding your core reason** [L1039.002]

1. Write down the first reason that comes to mind for starting the business, such as quitting a job, extra income, more family time, travel, paying off debt, or a better life for your family. [L1039.002]
2. For each reason, ask why you want that outcome. [L1039.002]
3. Repeat the question with each answer, going deeper, until you reach the underlying reason, such as health, sleep, or time for a relationship. [L1039.002]
4. Write the core reason down and keep it where you will see it when you need motivation. [L1039.002]

**Procedure: defining your reason for investing time and money in dropshipping** [L0519.003]

1. Write down on paper why you joined the program and why you are doing this, ideally the same week you start. [L0519.003]
2. Describe your dream outcome: what you want to do, how your daily life looks, where you live and what your number one goal is. [L0519.003]
3. Use the answers to find the path (skills, products, business model) that leads to that outcome, and revisit the notes when motivation drops. [L0519.003]

Keep your dreamline private. Sharing it with family or friends can lead you to water down your goals to match their opinions. The list only needs to motivate you. [L1039.011]

### Define what success means to you

Define your specific vision of success and what it means to you personally. Create a compelling picture of that outcome with no artificial limits. Self-knowledge of what you want is the prerequisite. [L0007.001]

Define success personally by the work that brings you fulfillment, not by arbitrary revenue targets. Consistent meaningful action aligned to your fulfillment makes you successful by your own definition, while business growth follows naturally. *Older advice, Source B 2021-2022.* [L0107.013]

Success is measured by growth, not absolute revenue. Growing from 0 percent to 1 percent month-over-month is measurable progress and success. [L0280.038]

A goal such as making more money is too vague to keep you going. A concrete picture of the lifestyle you want, such as time freedom, location freedom or financial independence, gives daily work a purpose. [L0519.004]

Set realistic goals that you actually want rather than abstract income targets. Money alone does not create happiness, so tie goals to what the business makes possible in your life. [L1043.002]

### Motivation and staying power

Use two motivators together: a pleasurable outcome to move toward (financial freedom, providing for family) and a painful situation to move away from. Keep both visible in daily thinking. [L1041.002]

A goal must be bigger than the obstacles it will meet, or the first hurdle will stop progress. Each goal should be compelling enough that you are willing to climb every obstacle on the way. [L1041.003]

Early on, expect delayed gratification: you will put in unpaid-feeling hours for two to three weeks or longer before results appear. Accepting that present work pays off later is what keeps people working when motivation drops. [L1039.003]

Your core reason is a tool to pull you back to work when distractions or low energy appear, because focus is what produces results. Successful people are not lucky by default; they use their reason to delay short-term comfort. [L1039.004]

A clear personal reason keeps attention on what matters and filters out distractions. Sustained focus is what turns effort into results. [L1043.001]

Feeling overwhelmed during the building process is normal. Understanding the specific end goal beyond abstract income targets, for example paying off debt in 2 years or funding a 3-month Europe trip next summer, provides motivation when daily tasks feel tedious. [L0251.054]

Passion is the ability to endure present pain because the end result is worth the effort, not a constant feeling of happiness. Purpose is what the world buys from you, while passion is what fuels you. [L0701.015]

Losing motivation after a fast start is normal for almost every business owner. Return to the original personal reason you started, such as extra income, leaving a job or more time for travel, and let that motivate you again. *Older advice, Source A 2020.* [L0961.083]

Spreadsheets and CSV files are only the necessary tools, not the purpose of the business. Keep the personal goal in view when the daily work feels heavy. *Older advice, Source A 2020.* [L0961.084]

**Procedure: staying motivated while working toward business goals** *(older advice, Source A 2022)* [L1092.104]

1. Write down the real personal goal the profit is for, such as a family trip, a move or school fees. [L1092.104]
2. Put that written goal somewhere visible while working. [L1092.104]
3. Measure progress against that goal, not only against a daily task count. [L1092.104]

### Money as a tool and scorecard

Money is only a tool. Treat it as a means to reach your goals, not as the goal itself. [L0831.008]

Treat money as a scorecard that shows whether the business is working and whether you are making the right decisions, not as the end goal. Pair the score with what it lets you do, such as a home, a trip, or a car, so progress feels meaningful. [L1039.039]

Running a business only to make money, with the plan to do something else later, keeps confidence low. A better approach is to move the business toward your own strengths so that it becomes the vehicle for what you really want. [L0701.008]

When profits rise quickly, a new seller can develop fear of losing the money and negative beliefs about earning it. Actively challenge these thoughts, because they can hold back decisions as income grows. [L1185.046]

Pursuing status symbols as a measure of success tends to produce frustration and unhappiness. Creative accomplishment toward goals you genuinely care about tends to bring lasting satisfaction and material results. [L1206.026]

### Time, money and income

Time and money are not directly linked. Income depends on the value, leverage, and scalability of what you sell, so avoid pricing only by the hour. [L0378.105]

Shift from payment for hours worked to payment for outcomes whenever possible. Income tied to results can keep growing, while hourly work caps your earnings. [L0378.106]

**Case: a 12-year-old tennis racket stringing service**

- Starting point: a used stringing machine bought for about $400 to $500. [L0378.108]
- Local clubs charged $12 to $15 per racket, and the stringer started at $25 with no experience. [L0378.108]
- A racket first took 45 to 60 minutes; after practice the time dropped to 16 to 20 minutes, with about 2 rackets per hour including setup. [L0378.108]
- This came to about $50 per hour at that age, more than most adults earned hourly. [L0378.108]
- Lesson: a focused skill, charged at a premium, can earn far more than the trade-time model. [L0378.108]

### Passion for the business, not the product

Passion for your specific niche category is not required. Focus on passion for building your business itself, which determines long-term success more than enthusiasm for the product. [L0031.005]

Channel your motivation toward building a successful business, watching it grow and executing well, rather than toward personal interest in the products you sell. [L0025.001]

Passion for the product is not necessary for a dropshipping business. Focus your passion on building the business, making sales and growing the store. [L0769.017]

Passion for the niche is secondary; passion for business growth and problem-solving is primary. Knowledge comes through experience, not prior expertise. *Older advice, Source A 2020.* [L0268.061]

Passion cannot be faked, so commitment is required for success, but it should be directed at the business itself rather than at the products sold. Direct your enthusiasm to building the business, not to the product category. [L0924.009]

The aspects of a store worth being passionate about are building a good-looking website, generating traffic, treating customers well, earning repeat purchases, and growing the store month after month. These areas determine long-term success regardless of the product. [L0924.010]

### Hobby versus income

A personal passion or hobby does not need to be your income source. If other products are significantly more profitable and easier to sell, building your business around those allows you to pursue your passion as a leisure activity separate from work. [L0025.002]

Your hobbies do not have to become your business. You can keep an interest purely for leisure and fund it from the profits of a business built on a better opportunity. [L0924.008]

**Case: a member with a scuba diving hobby**

- He first considered selling diving products because the hobby was strong. [L0924.007]
- He realized he did not need to earn money from the hobby, so he chose a product with better sales potential and easier selling. [L0924.007]
- He reinvested the profits into building the business and used part of the income to fund his diving trips. [L0924.007]
- Result: the hobby stayed a personal pleasure, while the business was built on a stronger opportunity. [L0924.007]

Keep several interests running at once, including one creative hobby with no income goal. Dropping an interest often makes the main work feel flat, while a hobby done only for enjoyment restores energy for the business. [L1343.039]

### Choosing what you will do with the income

Money is a tool for getting what you actually want, not a goal in itself. If you cannot name what you would do with the income, resolve that gap before you invest in scaling. Examples of outcomes to name:

- Giving back [L0701.003]
- Independence [L0701.003]
- Caring for family [L0701.003]
- Experiences [L0701.003]
- Becoming debt-free [L0701.003]
- Leaving a job you dislike [L0701.003]

## Write the 10-year vision and core values

### Why the vision comes first

- A business without a written destination and plan depends on luck to reach its goals. A clear end point gives the owner a roadmap to follow. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0439.002]
- Core business fundamentals such as direction, vision and core values guide almost every decision and come before marketing tactics. Most highly successful owners work from them in some form, so they should not be skipped. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0439.001]
- Skipping personal vision and development work because it seems unrelated to running the business is a mistake. Its absence tends to show up later as underperformance once the business is operating. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1256.003]
- A vision defines the destination, while the current situation is the starting point. It should cover both the personal life and the business, including why the business is being started at all. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1256.004]
- A clear picture of the end-point goal makes building the roadmap much easier. The picture should describe how both life and business look at that point, since the two become closely linked. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1256.008]

### What a vision is

- A vision is a detailed picture of the business and the owner's life once goals are reached, not just a statement of intent. A personal vision can be written in the same way alongside the business vision. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0439.004]
- Owners must be able to picture their success and believe they can reach their goals, because goals they do not believe in are rarely reached. This applies at any revenue level, whether the business earns 1,000 or 1,000,000 dollars per month. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0439.005]
- The vision does not require knowing how the goals will be reached. Describe the picture first, and work out the how later in the planning process. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0439.011, L1256.012]
- Set ambitious goals that feel slightly daunting, and do not let uncertainty about how to reach them stop the vision work. The question is where you truly want to be in order to feel happy, comfortable, and secure, not how to get there. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1256.012]

### Size, ambition and time horizon

- Use a 10-year time horizon for the vision. Use it for the vision exercise rather than a short one such as six months, because long-term vision comes first and significant results are built over longer periods; shorter-term targets follow from it. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0439.006, L1256.010]
- There is no right or wrong size for the vision. It can be a very large company or a small business. It should still feel challenging to achieve, because small long-term goals rarely keep an owner pushing day after day. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0439.007]
- A good vision excites the owner and may feel slightly scary. If the goals do not cause some fear or excitement, they are probably too small. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0439.008]
- Write ambitious, deliberately audacious goals rather than goals limited by what seems realistic today. [L1041.001]
- Set a time frame for long-term goals, for example reaching a strong financial position by about age 40 with five years of focused work. Include family and free time in the timeline. [L0832.020]

### Writing the vision

- Write the vision without outside influence and without copying example visions. Put down what the owner actually wants, without second-guessing it. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0439.029]
- There are no right or wrong answers in a personal vision. The goal can be very ambitious or modest, as long as it reflects what you want, and the document is private and only for you. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1256.013]
- The Vision on a Page worksheet contains a completed sample vision as a reference for how much detail each area can have. Write your own answers and do not copy the sample. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1256.016]
- The vision must be written down in the worksheet, not only held in mind, because the written document is used later as the reference for planning. Even a quick first draft counts as a starting point, and you should not wait for a perfect version before moving on. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1256.017]
- Keep the vision document simple and not overthought. Use a basic Pages or Word document, and write the answers from memory without external examples. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0439.027]

### The personal vision alongside the business

- A personal goal in the vision can be to live solely off passive income and no longer worry about business cash flow. The business is the means to fund this life. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0439.025]
- The vision should also describe your personal life in 10 years: family, location, living situation, travel and what you do daily. It can be a few short paragraphs or several pages, depending on how much detail you want. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0439.026]
- The personal life described in a 10-year vision, such as family, location, living situation and daily activities, often motivates more than the business figures do. Treat the business as the vehicle that funds that life, and connect the two explicitly in the vision. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0439.030]
- The personal life section can be short or long. A few short paragraphs (about five) or a detailed text of up to around three pages is acceptable, depending on how much you want to specify. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0439.031]
- In the early stage the solo owner is effectively the business, doing the website setup, supplier contact, and daily processes personally. Because of this, life and business become tightly connected, and the vision must address both. A team can be added later. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1256.009]

### Business vision document: ten steps

Keep the document simple. Use a basic Pages or Word document. [L0439.027]

1. Enter the business name, followed by the title "10 year vision". [L0439.027]
2. Write a quick overview of one or two paragraphs describing what the business looks like in 10 years and how success is measured, such as revenue, customers, lifestyle or side-hustle status. [L0439.027]
3. Describe the brand: how well known it is, who knows it, what people think of it, how many reviews it has and whether people recommend it unprompted. [L0439.027]
4. Describe the marketing: which channels are used, where marketing is done and how it performs. [L0439.027]
5. Describe the finances: revenue, profit, profit margin, who manages them, and what opportunities the profit creates. [L0439.027]
6. Describe the sales: who sells, how customers and prospects are reached, and how many people are involved in sales. [L0439.027]
7. Describe the customers: who they are, what they think of the business and what they do for it. [L0439.027]
8. Describe the operations and team: what the internal team looks like, which roles exist, where staff are based, and who manages the business day to day. [L0439.027]
9. Describe the personal life and lifestyle: daily life, family, location, travel and personal financial situation. [L0439.027]
10. List the 3 to 5 core values. [L0439.027, L0439.015]

### Vision on a Page: personal areas

- Picture yourself 10 years ahead and describe what your business and your life look like at that point. [L1256.011]
- Cover every area: business, finances, place of residence, family, number of children if desired, investments, what you do with business income, health, and significant relationships. [L1256.011]
- Take one to two days if needed; there is no need to rush. [L1256.011]
- Write one to two paragraphs for each area on the one-page vision worksheet. Length does not matter, only that every area is filled in. [L1256.011]

| Area | Guiding question |
|---|---|
| Business | What does the business look like? |
| Finances | What does your financial situation look like? |
| Residence | Where do you live? |
| Family | Where is your family, and how many children do you have if you want children? |
| Investments | What are you investing in? |
| Use of business income | What are you doing with the money your business makes? |
| Health | What does your health look like? |
| Relationships | What do your other significant relationships look like? |

Write one to two paragraphs per area. [L1256.014]

### Core values

- Choose 3 to 5 core values. More than five is not recommended, and the values should describe what the business stands for and what it should be known for. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0439.015]
- Build the business on five core values: take ownership and act on challenges directly instead of making excuses; work relentlessly to be the best at what you do; stay humble and keep improving by tracking marketing changes and sharing what works; treat teammates and customers as equals; and handle every dealing with the highest level of honesty, integrity, and transparency. These values apply at every revenue level, from a first sale to multi-million-dollar annual sales. [L0365.013]
- Define your values before choosing the audience you want to market to. A niche and a process that drain you are a sign that your values were never defined. [L0701.022]

### Daily review and visualisation

- Review the vision regularly, ideally first thing each morning, and visualize it with eyes closed. This helps on difficult days when doubt, criticism or setbacks occur. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0439.028]
- Revisit the written goals every morning so they shape the day's decisions. [L1041.001]
- Define a clear, personally meaningful vision of success before acting. [L1041.001]

### Review and revision

- Treat the vision as a starting point, not a fixed plan. It is expected to change as progress is made, and it should be reviewed at least once a year. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1256.015]

## Design your lifestyle, income target and personal finances

### Why a personal target comes first

- Building a business takes sustained effort before it generates income. Clarity on personal goals, called dreamlining, and regular reference to those goals keeps motivation up during unpaid phases. [L0276.048]
- Use this routine:
  1. Write down your ideal life: location, home type, vehicle, vacations, lifestyle. [L0276.047]
  2. Calculate the annual cost of that lifestyle. [L0276.047]
  3. Keep this document visible while you work. [L0276.047]
  4. When you do difficult, unpaid work, refer to these goals. [L0276.047]
  5. Remind yourself that you are working toward your defined life, not hoping the business works. [L0276.047]
- Goals without a cost figure give the business no money target and no clear measure of progress. Each goal should carry a cost. [L1043.007]
- Define your values and priorities before you cut time or cost. Freedom means something different for each person. Every plan involves trade-offs between free time and maximum money, more stuff and less stuff, spending and investing, and stability and uncertainty. [L0827.005]
- Answer these in writing, without copying what others do:
  1. What financial freedom means to you: a large comfortable house, a small studio, or something in between. [L1515.008]
  2. How much luxury you need in cars, hotels and possessions. [L1515.008]
  3. Whether you value location independence or a stable home base with room for guests and family. [L1515.008]
  4. Whether you want more free time or the maximum possible income. [L1515.008]
  5. Whether money should go to experiences now or to investments for later. [L1515.008]
- A billable career is capped by available hours. Profit above your current income is what allows growth beyond that cap. The first goal is to replace the income from your current job. [L0832.018]
- Source A used 100,000 dollars per month as a goal, tied to a life decision such as a partner leaving a job or starting a family. Set income targets as trigger points for life decisions, not as fixed dates. [L0832.019]
- Older advice (2021-22): Most drop shippers face money problems sooner or later. People usually fix financial control only after a problem forces them to, which costs more than planning ahead. [L0632.016]
- Older advice (2021-22): Knowing your numbers lets you step out of daily operations and reach higher levels of success. Without this knowledge you may reach some levels but stay stuck in daily firefighting. [L0632.017]
- Older advice (2021-22): Write down your money questions as homework:
  1. Set aside about five minutes. [L0632.018]
  2. Write down every question or problem you have about money and the numbers in your business. [L0632.018]
  3. Mark which common questions apply to you: product profitability, sales profitability, where money goes, supplier debt, hiring affordability, and selling the business. [L0632.018]
  4. Add any other financial questions or problems you need to solve. [L0632.018]
  5. Keep the list and use it to guide your next steps in financial learning. [L0632.018]
- Test your knowledge by trying to answer your key money questions within about ten minutes of looking at your records. If you cannot, you have a knowledge gap to close before you scale or sell. [L0632.019]
- Older advice (2021-22): Set the first goal around controlling your time and earning enough to cover current household costs, not around extreme wealth. A realistic income target that supports your current lifestyle is a sound starting point. [L1014.002]
- Every growing dropshipping store meets the same bookkeeping questions, such as where the profit went or why the bank balance is unexpectedly high. Meeting them is normal and does not mean you are doing something wrong. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1516.017]

### Dreamlining and the cost form

- Dreamlining turns your ideal life into a money target. Follow these nine steps:
  1. Decide the business type: a lifestyle business with freedom to work from anywhere, or a corporate-style company with employees and many stores. [L1039.006]
  2. Define where you want to live: city, state or region, and country. [L1039.006]
  3. Decide whether you want to rent or own, and whether you prefer a house or a condo. [L1039.006]
  4. Define your car: the specific model, its total price, and its monthly payments. [L1039.006]
  5. Define vacations: how many per year, how long each one lasts, and where you go. [L1039.006]
  6. List other lifestyle costs, such as dining out, a winter location, a personal trainer, or a chef. [L1039.006]
  7. Note any debt you want to pay down each month, or the amount you want to save each month. [L1039.006]
  8. Price every item with exact figures so you know the total cost of your ideal life. [L1039.006]
  9. Calculate how much you must earn to fund that life, then derive your goals from that number. [L1039.006]
- A shorter version of the same method:
  1. Write down what you want: where you live, what car you drive, what vacation you take, and what else you want. [L1043.003]
  2. Estimate what each item costs. [L1043.003]
  3. Add the costs to get the total the business must generate to pay for these goals. [L1043.003]
  4. Use the items as rewards that keep you working toward the target. [L1043.003]
- The 4-Hour Workweek Dreamlining Form is a template for listing personal life goals with their costs and turning them into a business money target. [L1043.004]
- A downloadable cost form based on Tim Ferriss's *The 4-Hour Work Week* lets you total the costs of your ideal lifestyle and the income needed to fund it. It replaces guessing with exact figures. [L1039.010]
- Set dreamline targets as a stretch beyond what you currently believe is possible, but not so far that almost nobody achieves them. Donating $5 million to charity within 18 months is unrealistic for nearly anyone. Upgrading to a comfortable apartment within a year is a fair stretch for someone living with parents. [L1039.007]
- Knowing your wants in advance lets you spend your earnings deliberately, and lifestyle items can serve as rewards. Owning a house is not an automatic goal just because income rises. The right choice depends on how much flexibility you value. [L1039.008]
- Before buying an expensive item such as a luxury car, write down why you want it, the exact model, its price, and its monthly payment. This prevents purchases you regret once income arrives. [L1039.009]
- Targets can follow different patterns, for example one two-week vacation per year or one one-week trip every quarter. Choose the pattern and destination, then price it so you know the annual travel budget your income must cover. [L1039.040]

### DEAL model and time-versus-money trade-offs

- Apply DEAL in this fixed order:
  1. Define: decide what you actually need from life, what you value, and what your priorities are. [L1515.001]
  2. Eliminate: cut every expense, possession, responsibility and daily task you can drop. [L1515.001]
  3. Automate: automate each task you cannot remove. [L1515.001]
  4. Liberate: outsource what still needs a human, once the process is clear and documented. [L1515.001]
- Eliminate in layers: reduce expenses first, then material possessions, then responsibilities, then daily routines. Whatever cannot be made smaller or removed should be automated before it is delegated. [L1515.002]
- Track time freedom as hours per week. Weekly working time fell from over 40 hours to under 20 hours after cutting responsibilities and automating tasks. [L1515.007]
- Money can buy time back in many ways, such as direct flights, ride-hailing, delivery, and cleaning services. Judge each time-saving purchase by comparing its price with the hourly value of your time. Otherwise convenience spending can quietly consume your income. [L1515.004]
- Count money and time together. A casual restaurant meal in a US city such as San Francisco can cost about 20-25 USD with tax and tip. Cooking at home also adds hidden time costs: grocery shopping, prep, cooking, cleanup and car errands. Compare both before choosing the cheaper-looking option. [L1515.005]
- Cost versus comfort, with examples:
  - Paying a few hundred dollars for a direct flight instead of a flight with two or three layovers is often worth it. A private jet at around 20,000 USD per hour is only worth it if your time is valued at that level. Compare the extra cost with the hours saved and the value you place on them. [L1515.047]
  - A traveler chose premium economy and paid cash for it instead of business class. The flight stayed comfortable and the large price difference went to investing. Choose a middle option when the top option costs more than the gain in comfort justifies. [L1515.048]
- Every purchase is a trade-off. You can have many things but not everything at once. Buying several major items can leave no cash for investing or emergencies, so decide deliberately which items deserve the money and time they consume. [L1515.009]
- Financing large purchases on long terms, such as 72- to 96-month car payments or leases and 30- to 40-year mortgages, can keep you paying for items long after you stop wanting them. Match the loan length to how long you realistically will keep the asset. [L1515.010]
- Possessions and downsizing: a single 60-liter carry-on duffel can hold everything a full-time traveler owns, including home, office and wardrobe. That minimalism means giving up items such as golf clubs or many pairs of shoes. Decide individually which possessions are essential before downsizing. [L1515.013]

### Location arbitrage and slow travel

- Living costs vary widely between cities and countries. Moving from a high-cost US city (about $4,000 to $6,000 per month in California) to a low-cost place (about $600 to $1,000 per month in Chiang Mai) can free roughly $3,000 per month for saving and investing. [L1126.010]
- Living costs of about 4,000-5,000 USD per month in a large US city fell to about 1,000-2,000 USD per month in places such as Chiang Mai, Eastern European cities or Prague. This roughly halves spending through location arbitrage. [L1515.006]
- Running a business from anywhere needs structure and routine. Location independence is built on a set daily rhythm, not on freedom alone. Slow travel and broadening your horizons are the other two elements that make this lifestyle sustainable. [L0926.001]
- Stability and uncertainty pull against each other, and frequent travelers often later buy a home base. A realistic plan combines slow travel with a stable base and chooses accommodation for comfort over months rather than the lowest nightly price. [L1515.011]
- Slow travel example: staying two to three months per location in monthly rentals of decent quality, with a couch, a TV and a separate bedroom, reduces logistics and fatigue compared with moving every two weeks. This costs more than the cheapest room but is chosen for comfort. [L1515.012]
- Living outside the US can make good private medical care affordable. It is often paid in cash at much lower cost than insurance-based care in the US. Include this when planning a move abroad. [L0827.008]

### Relocating with family

- A move abroad needs a strong personal reason. Copying others who went to the same place is not enough motivation to carry a family through difficulties. Every family member should agree to the move before it starts. [L1000.009]
- Relocating with children is harder when they lose close daily contact with grandparents and relatives. Before deciding, discuss the impact on each family member and agree how family back home will stay in touch. [L1000.073]
- Before travelling abroad with a family:
  1. Plan flights to the last detail. [L1000.010]
  2. Confirm visas and the exact dates you will be in each location. [L1000.010]
  3. Double-check those dates to avoid overstaying a visa, which can cause legal trouble. [L1000.010]
- Before arriving, research the new location thoroughly, including where the hospitals, shops and essentials are. A detailed destination guide with an interactive map helps. Local expat groups on Facebook are a good source of answers where no guide exists. [L1000.011]
- Check practical questions before committing to a location: nearby shops, how children will be cared for, daycare availability, the cost of a nanny, and seasons such as heavy rain or burning season that make living conditions poor. [L1000.012]
- A long-term base with several short retreats of two to three weeks suited one family better than living abroad permanently. Test a relocation before making it permanent, and expect to change plans if the first choice does not fit. [L1000.018]
- Case: Starting point was one online store, a part-time business run alongside a 9-to-5 job, and no team. The owner gambled on the business, quit the job, and within twelve months had three stores, two full-time virtual assistants, and a mastermind group. After the mastermind group formed at an industry retreat and the family sold its belongings, the family moved to Chiang Mai, Thailand, with a three-year-old daughter. The owner still answered store calls from about 4:30 a.m. Burning season made the air unlivable after about two months. The family moved on the spot to Phuket for about three weeks, found it too touristy, and returned to Australia to live with parents, about three months abroad in total. At this stage the owner hired a first virtual assistant, who managed the store day to day during a three-week trip to Bali, where the owner handled only minimal tasks. The result: three stores, two full-time assistants, and a home base with occasional mini-retirements. [L1000.019]
- Southeast Asia rules:
  - In Southeast Asia, food and water hygiene can be lower than in many home countries. Choose restaurants carefully. Do not drink tap water or brush teeth with it, and make sure children follow the same rule. [L1000.074]
  - Do not carry a family on a motor scooter in busy Southeast Asian towns. The risk to life is too high. Expect poor pavements, plan routes for pushing a stroller, and use safer transport for the whole family. [L1000.075]

### Income needed before quitting a job

- To find the income you need before leaving a job:
  1. Use the mindset and goal-setting lesson template to map the costs of your ideal life. [L0373.012]
  2. Look up real rent and list every other expense. [L0373.012]
  3. Add the totals to get the monthly income target. [L0373.012]
- Quit a job only after at least three consecutive months in which net profit from the business, not gross sales, equals or exceeds the salary of the job or jobs you leave. Months spread apart do not count. [L0373.013, L0471.049]
  - Source note: the 2024 version calls this true net profit and says to keep the job and build the store part time until the test is met. [L0471.049]
  - Source note: the 2020 version says to keep the day job until online business income matches job income for at least three months, without requiring consecutive months, and then to decide whether to quit, since you then have two incomes to choose from. Older advice (2020): [L0852.001]
- Example: if the jobs pay $3,000 per month and the store makes $3,000 net profit in each of three months in a row, the business has replaced that income. Total monthly income then reaches $6,000, and the owner can devote more time to growing the business. [L0373.014]
- Quit when the business reliably earns what the job pays, and factor in how much time you need for the transition. [L0829.028]
- Older advice (2022): An accountant can give peace of mind, but is optional. Many founders run as a sole proprietor first and add an accountant once revenue arrives. [L0963.151]

### Net worth and progress measures

- Net worth equals savings plus investments minus debt. It shows whether income is actually kept and built into assets, which makes it a better progress measure than gross revenue or salary. [L1126.001]
- Judge your business and personal finances by the profit you keep after all costs, not by gross revenue. Retained money is what builds wealth. [L0823.001]
- The Cashflow Quadrant from *Rich Dad Poor Dad* sorts income into four types: employee, self-employed, business owner and investor. The lesson states that employees and self-employed people control about 5% of total US wealth while making up about 95% of the population. The aim is to move toward owning a system, then owning investments. [L1126.012]
- Older advice (2021-22): Use the cash the business generates to fund long-term passive income, such as real estate or shares in other companies. Set a longer-term vision, for example five years, that defines what the investments look like and sets the income goals for the business. [L1480.033]

### Pay yourself first

- Move a fixed share of income into savings or investments before covering other spending. This makes saving automatic rather than dependent on what is left over. Saving whatever remains after spending usually leaves little or nothing. [L0823.002, L1126.005]
- Even a small amount such as $300 per month should go into savings as soon as income arrives, before any spending. A small fixed amount beats waiting for a larger surplus that may never come. [L1126.037]

### Profit First

- Older advice (2021-22): Profit First is a cash management method from Mike Michalowicz's book *Profit First*. It splits incoming revenue into percentages sent to separate accounts before money is spent, so each dollar has a predetermined purpose. [L1200.003]
- Older advice (2021-22): Pay the owner a regular salary from a planned account. This reduces burnout risk and makes personal income predictable. Irregular withdrawals create uncertainty and anxiety for the owner and household. [L1200.017]

### Saving and investing basics

- Do not try to time the market by predicting short-term rises and drops and buying or selling on those predictions. [L0823.003]
- Use low-cost index funds as the core of a long-term portfolio. [L0823.004]
- Plan to hold investments long term rather than trading in and out of positions. [L0823.005]
- Dollar-cost averaging means investing the same amount at regular intervals regardless of price. Over time this spreads purchases across high and low prices and reduces the risk of buying everything at a peak. [L0823.006]
- Limit speculative investments to 0% to 5% of the total investment portfolio. The rest goes into core long-term holdings. [L0823.007]
- Compare investments by the time and responsibility they demand, not only by expected return. A passive fund or loan platform can be worth a lower return if it removes the work of tenants, repairs and property management. [L1126.039]
- Do not sell investments during market downturns. [L0823.008]
- Selling in panic during a downturn turns temporary paper losses into permanent ones and usually means missing the recovery. Long-term investors should expect downturns and stay invested. [L0823.009]
- Investing books for beginners:
  - *Unshakeable* by Tony Robbins is a shorter version of his longer book *Money Master the Game*. *The Only Investment Guide You Ever Need* by Andrew Tobias is a classic introduction to low-cost index investing. [L1126.040]
  - When reading investment books, skip any section that promotes a specific portfolio or company the author has a stake in. Keep the general principles on low fees and long-term holding. *Money Master the Game* has a portfolio section of this kind that should be ignored. [L1126.041]
- Personal finance reading list: *The Millionaire Next Door* (habits of wealthy people who live modestly), *Rich Dad Poor Dad* (buy assets, not liabilities), *The Total Money Makeover* by Dave Ramsey (for getting out of debt), and *The Millionaire Fastlane* (test whether an expensive lifestyle purchase is worth it). [L0823.010, L1126.013]

### Debt and emergency fund

- Compound interest works in both directions. The mechanism that grows investments also makes credit card and other high-interest debt grow fast. Clear high-interest debt before serious investing begins. [L1126.004]
- Start investing only after debt is cleared and an emergency fund is in place. Build the plan around a fixed monthly amount. Investing money still needed for debts or bills is a common reason people sell in a panic. [L1126.035]
- Case: an online store owner with $70,000 in debt paid all of it off first and became debt-free. Afterward, their dropshipping stores reached about $10,000 in profit per month. Clearing debt before building a passive-income system was the key step. [L1126.038]
- Older advice (2021-22): Keep a rainy-day fund in a separate bank account. Months of runway equal the fund divided by monthly costs. This shows how long the business can cover its costs if income drops to zero. [L1334.013]
- Credit mistake: assuming that no credit or bad credit blocks business cards for good is wrong. Start with a business debit card, then a secured card. This builds credit that leads to better cards within one to two years. [L0699.016]

## Choose the kind of business and set honest expectations

### Decide the kind of business before anything else

- The mindset shift for scaling is moving from validating whether the business works to deciding how large you want it to become. [L0190.004]
- Decide early what kind of business you want. The three options are:
  - One you run for 2 to 3 years, then sell.
  - One that gives you freedom with a small team.
  - One that scales to 15 to 30+ people with multiple stores.

  This choice shapes every hiring and process decision, and every team member must understand the company's direction. [L0263.001]
- Brand building is a long-term commitment that does not suit everyone, because it requires consistent content and patience instead of quick sales. Decide whether you are willing to invest that time before you start. [L0487.065]
- Brand building is a process of several years, not a short project. Plan for patience, because partnerships and supplier improvements take time. [L0492.062]
- Building a high-ticket dropshipping business suits people who want to leave a job, gain control over their time and finances, and who are working long hours without a way out. [L1481.026]

### Choose your entry path

- Beginners should start with pump-and-dump products instead of supplements. Supplements compete against established brands that earn very large monthly revenues. Pump-and-dumps teach selling, testing and fast execution with less risk. [L0347.035]
- A pump-and-dump is a stepping stone, not a destination. Follow these steps to use it to build a long-term business: [L0347.034]
  1. Enter a pump-and-dump product to earn quick cash.
  2. Use the sales period to learn how ads work and how to test creatives.
  3. Reinvest the earned cash into a long-term brand.
  4. Level up by building the new brand with the skills and cash from the earlier product.
- Treating a pump-and-dump as a forever brand leads to getting stuck. Its role is to generate cash, teach advertising and fund a later brand. [L0347.036]
- Once a niche has produced a clear winner with strong revenue, it can be expanded into a brand with related products, such as adjacent fashion items. Expansion should be cautious because the niche may fade. [L1173.042]
- A Swiss brand for a wearable blanket started as dropshipping and moved to stock once early tests justified it. The sequence was:
  - Early tests at about 200 to 300 USD in ads produced about 3,000 CHF in sales in Switzerland, which justified the move to stock. [L0492.063]
  - In April 25 designs were bought, a shoot was held in mid-May and June, and about 500 units were ordered for the season at roughly 10,000 USD in product cost. [L0492.063]
  - Offers were set at 30 off, free local shipping and 2 to 3 day delivery, a buy-two saving of 14, and a code for 5 off a single item. [L0492.063]
  - Ads cost about 5,000 USD in winter for about 18,000 in November sales, roughly break-even overall. [L0492.063]
  - The first stock sold out by the end of December with 410 orders and no refunds. After new stock arrived, customers kept ordering without ads, bringing about 1,000 USD in revenue. [L0492.063]
  - Next steps were custom partner designs such as a football club logo for a one-time fee of about 10,000 USD for one year, Galaxus, and Germany and Austria. [L0492.063]
- Use the opportunity of a physical retail location to get access to brands and to learn which products customers return. Sell online only the brands whose return rates and manufacturer policies make online sales viable. *(Older advice, 2021.)* [L0801.168]

### Treat it as a business, not a side hustle

- Treat dropshipping as a real business that needs to be run, not as a side hustle. [L0631.008]
- Building a business takes months, not days. Be patient with the process and plan the timeline accordingly. [L0631.010]
- Each product test is part of running a real company with a revenue target in the six or seven figure range, not a quick product pump and dump. A disciplined process with patience is what separates a business from one-off wins. [L1124.016]
- Building a store is a long process that often takes weeks or months, not a few clicks. Consistent work over time is what produces results. *(Older advice, 2018.)* [L1109.067]
- Because dropshipping is widely promoted as a fast-money method, many newcomers quit once they see the real work involved. Building a real business with a professional approach is what lasts. *(Older advice, 2019.)* [L1069.076]
- Build for the long term rather than hoping for quick launches. A store can be built on the first launch, and results compound over the next decade. *(Older advice, 2022.)* [L0429.076]
- Many e-commerce owners become so absorbed in the business process or in finding a product that they neglect the fundamentals. Without knowing where the business and its products stand in the market and which customers to attract, the rest of the work is built on an unclear base. *(Older advice, 2021-22.)* [L0761.004]

### Build the first store before you expand

- Advice on expanding the business is best given after the first store works, because a new owner has no baseline to judge results. Start small, measure, and only then add stores, staff or agencies. *(Older advice, 2019.)* [L0467.073]
- Before opening another store, make a store with no sales profitable: [L0470.077]
  1. Ask friends and family to browse the store as a potential customer and give honest feedback on trust and what stands out.
  2. Make improvements based on that feedback.
  3. Focus on optimizing the ads and generating profitable sales.
  4. Open a new store only after this one is profitable.
- Starting a high-ticket dropshipping business from zero is a split effort. Beginners set up and launch their first store, while people who already run one focus on growing it. Anyone starting from scratch should work through setup and launch before moving to growth and marketing systems. *(Older advice, 2021-22.)* [L0438.001]
- Typical early challenges for new dropshippers are setting up the online store, finding reliable suppliers, and running profitable ads. Plan time and learning for each of these from the start. *(Older advice, 2019-22 source; undated in the course.)* [L0345.007]
- Dropshipping requires several skills at once: computer work, customer service and a business mindset, so the program covers more than a typical degree. Learning does not end, but the challenges change as the business grows. [L0832.025]
- Dropshipping requires several skills such as product finding, copywriting, video editing and Facebook ads, and the results move up and down over time. Expect setbacks and plan for them rather than assuming the business will be easy. [L1128.023]
- Dropshipping is simple but not easy at first. Each process becomes second nature with repetition, so early overwhelm is normal. [L0966.103]
- The first goal for beginners is experience and learning how to produce good creative content, not making money. Sales follow once the skills are developed. [L0820.039]
- When starting out, focus on gaining experience in posting and making content rather than on immediate profit or finding a winning product. Learning to produce good content is the prerequisite for later profits. [L0821.019]
- A new agency cannot reach 10 clients within two weeks. Early growth depends on building confidence and a portfolio first, often through free work for friends and family. [L0440.063]

### Plan the exit deliberately

- Build to sell: store multiples are currently at their highest levels in 12+ years. Selling a profitable store generates capital for scaling or starting new ventures. *(Older advice, 2022.)* [L0249.088]
- Start preparing for a sale early, because the sale process requires time whether or not a broker is used. Telling buyers that a trusted person runs the business and will stay is very valuable. [L0554.051]
- Expect buyers to push back on expenses and do not take this personally, because the store is a business deal rather than a personal project. Pushback on expenses is very common in negotiations. [L0554.050]
- Selling during a steep growth phase can feel like selling the farm, and some sellers regret it when the business doubles soon after. Choose the sale timing deliberately, based on the trend, rather than on emotion. [L1468.047]
- Choose the sale date by the revenue trend, not by emotion. If growth is still steep, a sale can leave money on the table, so waiting until the curve flattens may produce a better price. [L1468.055]
- A website is the output of a repeatable skill set, and that skill set is the real asset the operator keeps after a sale. Selling a single site while keeping the process allows the operator to build the next one. [L1468.054]
- Expect a business to change through growth stages. The main decisions at each stage are the ad budget, the number of products and the use of the existing audience. *(Older advice, 2022.)* [L0854.109]

### Read the economic and market conditions

#### Recession and downturns

- High-ticket dropshipping (products priced $1,000+) remains viable even during economic downturns and recessions. Wealthy consumers continue purchasing premium goods, and niches with lower competition but higher margins can grow rapidly. *(Older advice, 2020.)* [L0245.058]
- In the 2007-2008 economic collapse, the author built a high-ticket e-commerce business to multiple millions in revenue per year. Economic downturns create opportunity for those with online infrastructure while local competitors shut down. *(Older advice, 2020.)* [L0245.059]

#### Inflation and costs

- Inflation and rising costs are permanent reality. Expect products to become more expensive and adapt pricing. Launch now rather than waiting; 13 years of business proves successful adaptation is possible regardless of economic conditions. *(Older advice, 2022.)* [L0249.087]
- Plan for rising costs: inflation is expected to raise wholesale and retail prices, so keep a broad set of suppliers and monitor your margins regularly. *(Older advice, 2021.)* [L1090.142]

#### Supply chain

- Supply chain challenges are real but affect different niches to different degrees. If you work with 10+ suppliers, enough alternatives exist to remain profitable. Continue contacting suppliers while sales go through existing partners. *(Older advice, 2022.)* [L0249.086]
- A store owner used a supplier list of 20 to 25 approved brands, but a niche with only five suppliers was not enough. Broadening the niche one level brought the count to 20 or more, which allowed the store to move forward with a viable supplier base. [L0804.107]

#### Post-pandemic demand

- Post-pandemic, e-commerce adoption returned to its historical 20-year growth rate after the lockdown spike. Shopify hired for the spike rate (1,000 people added), then laid off 1,000 people (10% of workforce) on July 26, 2022 when growth normalized. This does not signal e-commerce decline; growth continues above pre-pandemic levels. *(Older advice, 2022.)* [L0272.071]
- Government cash payments to consumers, such as the 2020 US stimulus checks, can produce sales spikes for stores selling higher-priced products, because buyers spend money they did not expect to have. Such payments are not guaranteed and have no set date, so a store plan should not depend on them. [L1049.019]

#### Competition and market size

- Competition in dropshipping is rising slowly, not sharply, and many niches still have room. Businesses come and go, so a list of about 20 market ideas gives enough options to find a market with a suitable level of competition. *(Older advice, 2021-22.)* [L0771.059]
- New drop shippers entering a niche rarely harm a well-run store, because many of them rely on weak methods such as cold social ads or supplier directories. Judge your store by its own metrics rather than by how many new competitors appear. [L0965.092]
- A single profitable store represents a tiny share of total online buying, so one store does not put other businesses out of business. The US alone has about 330 million people, most of them online shoppers. *(Older advice, 2018.)* [L1085.097]

### Set realistic timelines and effort

#### Timeframes from the sources

Sources give different timeframes. Each applies under the condition shown.

| Timeframe | Applies when | Ids |
|---|---|---|
| Launch within 30 days | Full-time effort is possible. It is not required, and a slower start does not predict long-term success. *(Older advice, 2021-22.)* | [L0458.054] |
| Store live within about 30 days | The builder can work a couple of hours a day. Build carefully rather than fast. *(2023.)* | [L0964.145] |
| 2 to 3 weeks, or up to a year | Build time varies by person. Some members finished in two or three weeks, others took a year. *(Older advice, 2022.)* | [L0963.148] |
| 1 month to 2 years, with examples of 3, 6 and 12 months | Typical range for reaching a successful business. No one can reliably predict the exact timeline for a given operator. *(Older advice, 2021-22.)* | [L0419.009] |
| 6 to 12 months to first real results | Expect a plateau in between. The plateau is usually the hardest part. *(Source C.)* | [L0819.031] |
| 2 to 3 sales per week in the first month | The work is done consistently. This is not guaranteed. If the target is missed, look for optimizations in the Google Ads account instead of treating the launch as a failure. *(Older advice, 2021.)* | [L0801.181] |
| Up to $3,000/month profit within 30 days | Only if continuously optimizing (adjusting ads, improving product pages, responding to customer inquiries). This is not guaranteed and depends on execution, effort and market fit. *(Older advice, 2022.)* | [L0272.051] |
| 5 years to $300,000 to $500,000 profit | The worst-case scenario, and still valuable and life-changing. *(Source C.)* | [L0280.040] |

#### Effort and expectations

- Your timeline will differ from others, because your store, products, website and marketing style will be unique. The time needed to reach a goal is not a measure of success; what matters is whether you actually reach it. *(Older advice, 2021-22.)* [L0419.010]
- Compare only your own progress, because build time varies by person. *(Older advice, 2022.)* [L0963.148]
- Do not expect quick results. Dropshipping requires sustained effort over months and years before significant profits materialize. [L0280.041]
- Dropshipping and other e-commerce businesses usually take months of consistent work before results appear. Expecting quick riches is a common reason people quit, so plan for sustained effort over a long period rather than fast returns. [L0444.023]
- Building an e-commerce business requires long-term commitment. Even in the worst case, five years to $300,000 to $500,000 in profit is valuable and life-changing. [L0280.040]
- High-ticket dropshipping is simple as a model but not easy to execute. The first one to two years usually demand long hours and many failed experiments. Expecting quick results without this workload leads to discouragement. *(Older advice, 2021-22.)* [L1014.012]
- High-ticket dropshipping is not a quick fix or get-rich-quick plan. It requires sustained hard work, time and consistency, and it is not a fit for anyone who expects results in two weeks. *(Older advice, 2021-22.)* [L0771.055]
- There are no get-rich-quick shortcuts, and business requires consistent work. Success depends mainly on following a process consistently until it works, and quitting early is the most common reason for failure. *(Older advice, 2021-22.)* [L0458.056]
- There is no secret formula for dropshipping. Success comes from building basic skills, testing several products and offers, and scaling the one that wins. [L0519.001]
- Expect the first real results from e-commerce to take around six to twelve months, with a plateau in between. The plateau is usually the hardest part, and continued consistent work is what gets a store past it. [L0819.031]
- Low start-up cost does not mean easy. The low cost is offset by the work you need to put in. Business is not easy, and the people who succeed are the driven, focused, committed, and patient ones. *(Older advice, 2021-22.)* [L1480.032]
- Dropshipping is simple but not easy at first, so early overwhelm is normal. [L0966.103]

### Never promise income

- Do not promise or expect a fixed income from the business model. Results vary widely based on niche, optimization, traffic and effort, so set short-term goals such as a net profit target over the next 90 days. *(Older advice, 2021.)* [L0247.122]
- Income from this model varies widely, from zero to millions, depending on how you run the system, how fast you grow and how much you spend on ads as you scale. Do not plan around a fixed profit figure. *(Older advice, 2021.)* [L0968.104]
- There is no reliable average for first-year sales or profit in dropshipping, because results vary widely depending on the work and optimization done. Plan with your own test numbers rather than industry averages. *(Older advice, 2020.)* [L0551.061]
- Avoid promising revenue amounts by a fixed date, since launch timing depends on many variables such as account flags and optimization delays. Most stores need several weeks of setup and data before results can be predicted. [L0471.052]
- Revenue goals such as $1M are possible but never guaranteed, and income claims should not be made to customers or prospects. Set goals based on your own tracking, not promises. [L0808.051]
- A six-figure sales month is gross sales, not net profit. A two-person family store launched in 2023 reported its first six-figure sales month in May 2024, followed by six-figure months in June and July 2024. The owners had a steep learning curve before reaching them. [L0373.024]

### Be skeptical of online claims

- Be skeptical of people claiming success online. Verify that they can actually prove their business makes money, because many large creators promote products they don't actually use or believe in. [L0280.042]
- Do not trust online claims that promise a fixed income within a fixed time. Profit timing varies with the product, market, and the store's own setup, so such promises are usually unreliable. *(Older advice, 2021-22.)* [L0438.011]
- Be sceptical about claims made online about suppliers refusing new retailers. Such claims often describe a specific period or industry, such as fitness products during the pandemic, that does not apply broadly. *(Older advice, 2021.)* [L0962.144]
- Most people who start an online store never get to the stage of having traffic, and the odds of success improve a lot once a store gets its first real sales. Opening an empty Shopify store is a very small commitment, so an alleged 98% failure rate is not a reliable measure. *(Older advice, 2021.)* [L1090.141]

### Use AI as leverage, not as passive income

- Traditional dropshipping relied on guesswork and hustle, while AI dropshipping relies on systems and leverage. The seller becomes an operator who uses technology to outpace competitors rather than only a seller. [L1459.016]
- AI amplifies entrepreneurs rather than replacing them. Sellers who use AI correctly are expected to outcompete those who do not. [L1459.017]
- This is not passive income. Passive income means money from investments such as rental property or dividends that needs no daily work, and a dropshipping business does not provide that on its own. It demands a lot of work early on. *(Older advice, 2021-22.)* [L1480.031]

## Build beliefs, identity and self-image

### The chain from belief to result

- Your beliefs shape your results more than the actions you take right now. Results follow a chain in which beliefs shape thoughts, thoughts create feelings, feelings lead to actions, and actions produce results, so changing outcomes means changing the underlying beliefs. [L0831.001]
- The chain that produces results runs Programming, Thought, Feeling, Action, Result. Programming is the set of automatic beliefs and conditioning from the past, and it shapes every thought that follows. To change results you must change the programming at its root, not only the tactics you apply. [L1513.001]
- Most people try to improve results by adding skills or tactics while keeping the same underlying programming. This produces the same outcomes because actions still come from faulty automatic thoughts. Thoughts often arrive before any conscious decision, so they need deliberate review. [L1513.002]
- Skills such as niche selection, store building, copywriting, negotiation and paid advertising are actions that come out of beliefs. Beliefs about how to think as an entrepreneur matter more than any single skill, because faulty beliefs lead to poor actions even when the skills are sound. Inspired ideas also come from programming, so better beliefs produce better ideas. [L1513.004]
- Things have no meaning in themselves, because meaning is assigned by the person interpreting them. Only a few things are facts, while most of the rest is interpretation. Choose interpretations that empower you to act rather than ones that limit or discourage you. [L1513.026]
- Pain and pleasure are the primary drivers of human behavior. Master the ability to direct them consciously by creating deliberate mental associations: link discomfort with behaviors you want to eliminate, and positive reward with the outcomes you want to create. This conscious direction, rather than being unconsciously driven, is foundational to business success. [L0007.027]
- Treat your mindset as the foundation of the business: protect it from negative influences and sharpen it with deliberate practice. Use that mindset to guide every decision you make in the business. [L0631.015]

### Mindset as a limit on results

- In e-commerce, mindset usually limits results more than technical knowledge or strategy does. When execution stalls despite knowing the steps, the cause is often a mental habit rather than a missing skill. [L1042.001]
- Mindset is described as roughly 80 percent of what separates operators who succeed from those who do not. Attitude and the way problems are interpreted therefore matter more than tactics alone. [L1445.013]
- Tactics such as the latest ad targeting or platform strategy change quickly, while mindset determines long-term results. The more time you invest in personal growth and a business mindset, the less the tactics matter. [L0701.024]
- Cautious habits picked up from a risk-averse upbringing conflict with e-commerce, which rewards comfort with uncertainty. Expect the business to repeatedly test that comfort zone. [L1042.002]
- Early business mindset traps include thinking short-term, being easily distracted, expecting fast results, and expecting something for nothing. Recognizing these traits early and changing how you approach results is necessary before any success appears. [L1394.018]
- Limiting beliefs about how far a product can grow can hold back revenue even when the numbers are strong. Speaking with experienced operators can help break through a growth plateau. [L1127.037]
- Your price is limited by how much you believe you deserve. If you struggle to raise prices, examine your self-worth and your belief about the value you deliver. [L0378.107]
- Money flows along the path of least resistance, and limiting beliefs about earning money act as resistance that blocks it. Removing negative emotional attachments to money makes income easier to attract and keep. Money itself has no agenda or judgement about the owner. [L1513.014]
- Feelings of inferiority come mainly from measuring yourself against other people's standards, not from actual gaps in skill. Measuring yourself against your own standard, and accepting that everyone is unique, removes most of that feeling. [L1206.013]
- Negative beliefs are usually built through repetition and emotion, so they are weakened by stronger, more consistent beliefs backed by emotion. Vividly picturing a desired outcome repeatedly generates good emotions in the same way that worry generates bad ones. [L1206.012]
- A salesman was consistently earning about $5,000 a year regardless of how good or poor his territory was or how commissions changed. A consultant found the cause was the salesman's own belief that he was a $5,000-per-year man. When he was given a poor territory he worked hard to reach the figure, and in a good territory he slowed down once he got close to it. The case shows how a self-evaluation can limit earnings more than external conditions do. [L1206.043]

### Self-image and identity

- Your self-image, meaning the mental picture you hold of the kind of person you are, sets the boundaries of what you can accomplish. Results and behavior tend to match that picture, so lasting change usually requires updating the self-image first. [L1206.001]
- Positive thinking aimed at a single goal fails when it contradicts a negative belief about yourself. Such thinking only works once the underlying self-belief has shifted. [L1206.002]
- Identity precedes results: decide who you want to become, label yourself as that person, and think, talk and act in line with it. Each daily thought, word and action is a vote for the person you are becoming. [L1041.012]
- Every thought, word, and action registers as a vote on the person you are becoming. Develop habits, through thoughts, words, and deeds, that align with your desired identity. [L0007.008]
- Build a deliberate identity that drives your behavior:
  1. Decide clearly on who you want to become. [L0007.007]
  2. Identify and explicitly label that identity. [L0007.007]
  3. Take consistent actions that reinforce it. [L0007.007]
  4. Actively seek and collect proof of the identity. [L0007.007]
- To take on the role of a designer, first learn beginner Photoshop techniques such as blending, then complete small finished projects. Each completed project serves as concrete proof that the new identity is real, which reinforces the behavior. [L1041.034]
- Adopt a gaming mindset toward business and personal development. Choose your desired character, continuously improve your skills, and understand that accumulating more skills, relationships, and resources unlocks larger opportunities and more significant games to play. [L0007.017]
- Think like a successful entrepreneur by asking what a successful person would do if they started from zero without that money, and reverse-engineer a lazy person's habits to find the opposite behavior. Successful people delay gratification and avoid overspending and emotional decisions. [L1041.024]
- Ignore opinions from people who have not done what you are trying to do. Their doubts often reflect their own beliefs about what is possible, not the reality of your goal. [L1041.025]
- Living the benefits of a successful business before the money arrives supports confidence. Waiting for the money first and only then trying to enjoy its benefits tends to erode confidence. [L0701.001]
- Confidence in the sense of self-efficacy means believing you can succeed in specific situations and believing in your own self-worth. It is a belief, not the amount of activity you produce. [L0701.012]
- When your business persona conflicts with your real self, stress builds and confidence falls. Aligning both into one consistent identity restores confidence without needing tricks or forced self-presentation. [L0701.023]
- Treat yourself with the same kindness you would show a good friend: avoid harsh self-criticism and reward good work with something positive. [L1041.016]
- When you feel guilt about an action your reasoning says is not wrong:
  1. Stop and name the cause of the feeling. [L1206.042]
  2. Examine that cause in detail and test whether it holds up. [L1206.042]
  3. If it is absurd, state clearly why and reject it. [L1206.042]
  4. Whenever a similar irrational thought comes up later, examine and reject it the same way. [L1206.042]

### Role models

- Identify people you admire, study their mentality, habits, and behaviors deeply, and emulate their actions and approaches. This accelerates skill development. [L0007.009]
- Choose role models whose behavior you can copy, such as how they outsource tasks and focus on their strengths, and deliberately emulate their habits and ways of thinking. [L1041.013]

### Acting as if

- Belief in yourself is foundational to action. If genuine belief is absent, act as if you have it until the belief catches up. You are not your past, so examine whether old beliefs still serve you. [L0007.003]
- Act as if you already hold the role you want, even before you feel it or have achieved it. Look for small pieces of evidence that support the new identity so the behavior compounds. [L1041.004]
- A writer decided to act as if she already had the ability she suspected she had, instead of acting tentatively. Within a year, her output and sales as a writer had increased many times over, and she discovered a talent for public speaking that she had never used. The change came from a deliberate decision to act on an assumed ability before any proof appeared. [L1206.047]
- Two psychologists asked 45 men hospitalized with neuropsychiatric conditions to retake a personality test while answering as a typical well-adjusted person would. Three-fourths of them scored better, and some changes were large. Imagining the role of a well-adjusted person was enough to shift how the men answered and how they felt. [L1206.041]
- In a demonstration, a participant's grip strength measured 100 pounds on a dynamometer in his normal state. After a suggestion that he was much stronger than he had ever been, the same grip reached 125 pounds, without any change in physical capacity. The example shows how a self-imposed limit can hold back ability that already exists. [L1206.038]

### Core beliefs: the ten beliefs

#### Commitment and responsibility

- Commit fully to succeeding rather than only wishing for success. Commitment is the belief that separates operators who make it from those who only hope. [L0831.004]
- Belief 1: Commit to the outcome instead of only wanting it. Saying I want something declares that you do not have it and keeps the lack in place. Commitment treats the outcome as already decided and keeps you moving through setbacks. [L1513.006]
- A commitment test: imagine an opportunity with a 99.9 percent chance to return 10 times a minimum investment of 20,000 dollars, and then imagine the 0.1 percent failure happens and the money is lost. If you would still keep pursuing your goal, your commitment is strong. Commitment can be built up over time through repeated practice. [L1513.007]
- Take full responsibility for everything that happens in your business, both good and bad. Blaming markets, suppliers, or others removes your ability to change the outcome. [L0831.005]
- Belief 2: You are 100 percent responsible for everything that happens in your business, including the actions of staff. If a team member makes a mistake, the owner who hired and trained them shares the responsibility and must fix the training, the procedure or the hiring. Full responsibility gives full control over outcomes. [L1513.008]

#### Learning and growth

- The more you learn, the more you can earn. Continuous learning is treated as a direct driver of income. [L0831.006]
- Belief 3: The more you learn, the more you earn, and learning never ends because markets and platforms keep changing. Dismissing a new channel because you dislike it can cost a lot, as many large social platforms became major sales channels after early owners ignored them. Keep learning current platforms even when they feel unattractive. [L1513.009]
- The more you earn, the less you need to work. Growing income should eventually reduce the hours you personally put in. [L0831.007]
- Working harder as the only way to grow a business leads to burnout and a hard ceiling on income. Hours in a day are limited, so the business must be restructured through staff and systems before growth can continue. [L1513.012]
- Growth can come from two sources: repeated painful trial and error, or a sudden shift in understanding when a belief clicks. Consciously reflecting on a new belief until it feels true can lock it into the subconscious and change behavior faster than years of trial and error. Review the belief list regularly to give this process time to work. [L1513.005]
- Personal growth happens in two ways: through painful experience or through sudden moments of clarity. Both paths produce learning, and the second can come faster. [L0831.003]
- Adopt a growth mindset: treat every result, good or bad, as information for the next step rather than a final verdict on your ability. Failure is a lesson, not an identity. [L1039.028]
- Do not judge yourself by old beliefs, do not accept others' feedback as the final answer, and learn from experiences yourself. Difficult results still carry useful information if you look for it. [L1039.029]
- A fixed mindset treats a failure as proof of inability and leads to giving up. Avoid it by treating every failure as a lesson and a cue to improve. [L1043.012]
- Test every limiting belief by asking whether it is useful for growth, not whether it is true. Discard beliefs that do not serve progress. [L1041.005]

#### Customers, money and tools

- Expect that customers will pay you for real value. Build the business on the assumption that people are willing to buy. [L0831.009]
- Belief 5: Treat money as a tool. Spend to make money, and do not let emotional ties to money prevent investment that has a proven return. [L1513.015]
- Belief 6: People pay you money, not PayPal, Stripe or any other tool. Keep customer psychology at the centre of planning, marketing and offers. [L1513.017]

#### Think big

- Think big when setting goals and planning the business. Small thinking limits the size of the store and the decisions behind it. [L0831.010]
- Belief 7: Always think big instead of playing it safe, since aiming high requires similar energy as aiming low. Set goals that would be worth reaching if only part of them succeeds. [L1513.019]

#### Receiving

- Be open to receiving money, help, and opportunities. Resistance to receiving blocks growth even when the business is working. [L0831.011]
- Belief 8: Learn to receive well. Accept gifts, money and compliments by looking the giver in the eye and saying thank you, without deflecting. Practising this regularly builds receiving as a habit and does not block others from giving. [L1513.020]

#### Play to win

- Play to win instead of playing not to lose. A defensive mindset makes you avoid the bold moves that growth requires. [L0831.012]
- Playing not to lose, such as aiming only to cover bills or break even, keeps a business small and makes the owner give up quickly under pressure. Starting a business is emotionally and mentally demanding, so it is worth aiming to win clearly rather than just to survive. [L1513.021]
- Belief 9: Play to win instead of playing to not lose. Set ambitions that go beyond covering costs, because the effort of building a business is similar either way. [L1513.022]

#### Keeping your word

- Always keep your word to customers, suppliers, partners, and yourself. Reliability is part of the business's long-term value. [L0831.013]
- Belief 10: Keep your word, because integrity means that what you say and what you do stay whole. Every unfinished promise to yourself keeps an open loop in your mind that fills your thoughts. Each broken promise also lowers how much you trust yourself to follow through. [L1513.023]
- To close open loops and stay in integrity, follow these two options for each commitment you have made:
  1. Complete it when you said it would be done. [L1513.024]
  2. If you no longer want it, cancel it clearly and explicitly, telling yourself it is over. Then focus on the next priority and avoid keeping unfinished goals in the background. [L1513.024]
- Integrity can be scored as a rough percentage showing how often you keep the commitments you make to yourself and others. Honest first ratings tend to come out low, with many people scoring below 50 percent, so tracking the score over time shows whether you are improving. [L1513.028]

### Effort and hustle

- Hustle is the only success factor you fully control, so it is the most important lever in the early stage of a business. [L0829.022]
- Hustle requires courage, confidence, self-belief and self-determination to keep working until the opportunities you want appear. [L0829.023]
- Hustle is effort you control, combining daily commitment with a real desire for the result. It means working consistently rather than only harder for a short time. [L0832.024]

### Gratitude and confidence exercises

- Daily gratitude exercise for quick confidence:
  1. Set a timer for two minutes at most. [L0701.025]
  2. List five to ten things you are grateful for, including ordinary things such as family, food, good people, or the electricity that powers a light. [L0701.025]
  3. Do this every day, for example after waking up. [L0701.025]
  4. Repeat the confidence rating from 1 to 10 afterwards and compare it with the earlier score. [L0701.025]
- Gratitude shifts the mind from a scarcity mindset to an abundance mindset, which makes opportunities around you easier to notice. In the test, most people rose one or two points on the 1 to 10 confidence scale. [L0701.026]
- Income or revenue often starts to rise about six to eight weeks after consistent work on the mindset and self-alignment steps, sometimes doubling or tripling in that period. [L0701.028]
- Build confidence with the Sacred Six:
  1. Forgive yourself for past mistakes. [L0007.010]
  2. Commit to doing what you say you will do. [L0007.010]
  3. Rack up wins, no matter how small. [L0007.010]
  4. Remove negativity from your environment. [L0007.010]
  5. Visualize your desired outcomes. [L0007.010]
  6. Seek knowledge continuously. [L0007.010]
- Sacred Six daily practice for building self-confidence, done each morning:
  1. Forgive yourself for yesterday and accept that you are human. [L1041.017]
  2. Commit only to what you say you will do, then do it, so you can trust yourself. [L1041.017]
  3. Collect small wins through the day instead of counting losses. [L1041.017]
  4. Remove negativity from your environment, such as news and negative content, and process negativity without letting it consume you. [L1041.017]
  5. Visualize your goals in as much detail as possible, so the brain filters for relevant opportunities. [L1041.017]
  6. Seek more knowledge and apply what you learn. [L1041.017]
- Write a short reminder into each calendar block that states the kind of person to be on that day or a brief affirmation. Reading it at the start of the block helps shift attention to the intended mindset. [L1181.032]
