- Scaling sales without growing operations and finances at the same pace usually leads to burnout or business failure. Growth holds only when the back end can carry the extra volume. (Older advice, Source B, 2021-22.) [L1334.001]
- An owner who still answers customer questions, fixes checkout or product pages, handles stock-outs or updates prices cannot scale. Leaving daily operations is the precondition for growth. (Older advice, Source B, 2021-22.) [L1334.002]
- The owner ladder has five levels (Source B, 2021-22): [L1334.003]
  1. Doing all the work yourself. [L1334.003]
  2. Paying others to do it. [L1334.003]
  3. Managing those who do the work. [L1334.003]
  4. Managing those who work on the business. [L1334.003]
  5. At the top, only collecting profit distributions. [L1334.003]
- The lesson set the distribution rhythm at about once per quarter. (Older advice, Source B, 2021-22.) [L1334.003]
- Working in the business means performing tasks: customer service, website and product work at the base, and marketing such as SEO, PPC and email at the higher level. Working on the business means building a machine that runs without you, including hiring, creating systems and managing finances. (Older advice, Source B, 2021-22.) [L1334.004]
- One person cannot run marketing, operations and finance at scale, so outsourcing work such as SEO and PPC is required. An HR manager can handle hiring, an operations manager can build processes, and a CFO can manage finances if the owner is weak in those areas. (Older advice, Source B, 2021-22.) [L1334.005]
- Owners may keep tasks they enjoy and do well, such as customer calls or running PPC, as long as the choice is deliberate. The owner can sit anywhere between doing basic tasks and doing none, but not because they believe they cannot delegate. (Older advice, Source B, 2021-22.) [L1334.006]
- The owner stays ultimately responsible for the business and should understand what each person does. Strong hires should then be trusted to do their work without constant checking, because over-supervising holds them back. (Older advice, Source B, 2021-22.) [L1334.016]

### Hiring order and roles

- Map all roles first. A solo owner fills every box of the company's role chart at once and switches roles all day. Mapping the roles shows which boxes the first hires should fill. (Older advice, Source B, 2021-22.) [L1334.007]
- Do not look for a mini-version of yourself to hire. Give each hire one main role with at most one or two side roles, so that no one is stretched too thin. (Older advice, Source B, 2021-22.) [L1334.008]
- Hire maintenance roles such as customer service and order handling before growth roles such as marketing. Growing sales into an unready operation multiplies problems: 10 times the sales means 10 times the problems. (Older advice, Source B, 2021-22.) [L1334.009]
- Scale hiring one role at a time. Build product listers, then customer service, then operations. This prevents chaos and lets you perfect each function before adding the next layer. [L0263.049]

### Capacity planning and staff budget

- Once a business is in the growth phase, operating capacity rather than sales is the main limit on growth. Ask how many orders and sales the team and systems can absorb before tasks slip, such as supplier orders that are forgotten or customers chasing late orders. (Older advice, Source B, 2021-22.) [L1334.010]
- Aim for about 80% capacity utilization, not 100%. Full capacity burns out staff and leaves no time for improvement, and running at the limit throttles growth. (Older advice, Source B, 2021-22.) [L1334.011]
- In seasonal niches, save surplus in the staff account during the peak season to pay for the slow season. Avoid laying off people in slow months; give them other useful work instead. (Older advice, Source B, 2021-22.) [L1334.012]
- Hire before you need the person, because recruiting and training take longer than expected. Allocate more to staff than the current need requires, so there is a buffer, since new hires need time before they add results. (Older advice, Source B, 2021-22.) [L1334.014]
- Budget committed to staff should be spent on hires, not left idle. Money that sits unused while the plan says it is for staff means the plan is not being followed. (Older advice, Source B, 2021-22.) [L1334.015]
- Hire into the staff allocation on a schedule, not on impulse. Check the budget against real revenue and remaining staff funds before adding any new role. (Older advice, Source B, 2021-22.) [L1334.042]
- Plan each hire as an ongoing role that may last for years, not as a short placement. Repeatedly rehiring costs time and money and is one of the worst outcomes in team management. (Older advice, Source B, 2021-22.) [L1054.003]

### Leadership, performance and trust

- Evaluate leadership on two dimensions: Performance (skills, results on the job) and Trust (integrity, reliability, character). High performers with low trust are toxic and damage culture long-term. Medium performers with high trust are more valuable than high performers with low trust. [L0263.035]
- Organizations over-measure performance (thousands of metrics) and under-measure trust (zero metrics), which causes them to promote toxic people. Reverse this: make trust equally important. [L0263.035]
- Allow creative team members to be creative without micromanaging. Micromanagement prevents you from getting their best work and contradicts why you hired them. [L0260.023]
- If you want to go fast, go alone; if you want to go further, go together. Lean on your team's perspectives and include diverse viewpoints when developing brand strategy. [L0260.022]

### Treating staff with respect

- Even without a formal employment contract, once someone works for you, you are their employer, and they experience the relationship that way. Treat outsourced staff as you would a local employee, with support and development. (Older advice, Source B, 2021-22.) [L1054.002]
- Many owners fail at outsourcing because they value a remote assistant less when their pay is low compared with local wages in their own country. That attitude leads to weak effort, poor results and little success with outsourced staff. (Older advice, Source B, 2021-22.) [L1054.001]
- Greet your team at the start of each day, even when you have no task to discuss. Ignoring staff creates the same poor impression as a boss who walks past the team without acknowledging anyone. (Older advice, Source B, 2021-22.) [L1054.021]
- Hold performance reviews that are positive and focused on development. Name what the person does well, then identify weaknesses together and ask how the work could be improved and what they need. (Older advice, Source B, 2021-22.) [L1054.027]
- Harsh threats, such as telling someone to fix their act or be fired tomorrow, do not produce improvement. The person never gets a real chance to explain what they find difficult. Give them a chance and work with them using empathy. (Older advice, Source B, 2021-22.) [L1054.028]
- Apply empathy in every interaction with staff. Imagine being in their position and ask whether you would be happy with how you treat them. If the answer is no, change your approach. (Older advice, Source B, 2021-22.) [L1054.040]
- As soon as you hire staff, you are a leader, and your example sets the tone for the team. A poor manager cannot expect good work from the team, so treat people with the respect you would want yourself. (Older advice, Source B, 2021-22.) [L1054.041]
- Treat other members with respect and avoid bickering, because conflict does not help results. Address problems that disrupt the community directly, and keep the focus on positivity, helping others and results. [L0365.011]
- Success is a team effort, so no single person is responsible for it and every contribution matters. Do not look down on others; everyone is valued equally regardless of their stage. [L0365.012]

### Honesty and values in the business

- Do not misrepresent your experience, skills or how long you have been doing business. This applies to customers, suppliers and community members alike. [L0365.004]
- Lying about your skills, experience or track record to sell products or to create extra income in a community is a serious mistake. Such misrepresentation tends to be discovered, and those involved can be removed from the community. [L0365.005]
- Handle every business issue with honesty and transparency, both with colleagues and with customers. Ethical integrity is the highest standard for long-term success. [L0365.008]
- Stay humble and keep learning, because there is always a way to improve regardless of revenue level. Track changes in marketing continuously and share what works. [L0365.010]

### Family and life outside the business

- Schedule regular calls with family back home, including grandparents, so children stay connected and relatives are not left out. Plan activities together to make the time away worthwhile. [L1000.016]
- Keeping store phone duty while living in a different time zone meant waking at 4:30 a.m. every day to take calls. Hand over phone handling to a trained assistant before a long trip abroad. [L1000.017]

## Think creatively and solve problems

### Diagnosing a problem before fixing it

- Find the root cause before working on secondary issues such as page layout or copy. If the root cause stays unsolved, changes to other parts of the business bring little benefit. [L1254.001]
- Treat a business problem like a blocked stream: identify what blocks the flow, remove that block, and the rest of the business starts working. Judge which other changes are worth making only after that. [L1254.002]
- Changing five or more things at once hides which change actually fixed the problem and often leaves the real cause in place. Test the most likely root cause first. [L1254.003]
- When a plan does not work, adjust it, address the problem directly and push through instead of holding on to the original plan. [L0829.019]
- When a store is struggling, take responsibility for the cause, such as weak ads or too few suppliers, instead of blaming outside factors. Fixing the controllable causes is the main lever. [L0832.023]
- Successful entrepreneurs do not panic when a problem appears. They stay calm, break the problem into steps and solve them one by one. [L0887.017]
- Long hours do not automatically produce results. What counts is working on the right tasks. If effort brings no progress, check whether the problem lies in product choice, ad angle or offer rather than in how hard you work. [L0887.013]
- Limit the time spent on a single problem and keep working on the business in parallel. Spending hours each day on a payment dispute reduced the time and focus available for the product and the company. [L1128.019]
- A common beginner mistake is panicking when something breaks, then sending many messages to coaches or the community and waiting for replies while doing nothing. Asking AI first and trying to solve the problem yourself avoids this loss of time. [L0887.019]

Handling a problem in your business:

1. Describe the problem to AI with the platform, theme, exact symptom and any numbers.
2. Try to understand the answer and apply the suggested fix.
3. If you are still stuck after this, post your question in the community or ask a coach, with what you already tried.

This order builds independence and speeds up your growth. [L0887.018]

Example: a reset plan for a stalled store, given by AI, covers 3 days: launch 1 new product per day, test at least 3-5 creatives per product and spend small amounts purely to collect data. Kill products quickly when they show no traction. [L0887.015]

### Using AI as a thinking partner

- Talking through a problem in writing with AI forces you to organise your thoughts and reveals the bigger picture. Use AI to clarify decisions before acting on them. [L0887.029]
- AI can be used for personal problems too, such as low energy or demotivation at work. Describing the situation to it gives you an outside perspective you may not see yourself. [L0887.012]
- Use AI tools only at named steps where they measurably shorten the task, and state for each step when AI should be used. Applying AI to every step without that decision adds effort without saving time. [L0555.012]
- Long AI chats drift and forget earlier brand instructions. Keep one restartable thread that holds the primer and the prompts, and re-prime it when the output stops matching the brand. [L0567.042]
- Use Google Gemini instead of ChatGPT to review policy pages for Google Ads and Merchant Center compliance. Gemini is Google's own model and is the better fit for checking content against Google's own policy rules. [L0724.011]

### Challenging AI recommendations

- Challenge AI-generated SEO and strategy recommendations before acting on them, especially big decisions about content direction. Ask for the strongest argument for and against each suggestion, and expect to drop some items from the list. This matters most for premium brands, where AI-written content can drift toward a cheap tone. [L1269.001]
- Use this prompt to test a recommendation:

```
Before we act on this recommendation, give me the strongest steelman argument for doing it and the strongest steelman argument against it.
Then give an honest verdict: is this necessary for our goal right now, and which items from the list would you cut?
Flag anything that does not fit our brand positioning, price level or target customer.
Keep the verdict short and tie every action to either a new visitor or a new customer.
```

[L1269.002]

- The same AI audit run repeatedly can produce a different priority order each time. Treat its ranking as a starting point and not a fixed plan. Reframe every suggestion by asking whether it will actually move traffic or revenue in the next weeks. [L1269.003]

### Approaching every new problem with fresh thinking

- Every new product, market and advertisement is a new problem. Solutions that worked before cannot be reused without copying, and success should push you to search again rather than to reopen old files. [L0330.060]
- Analysis is a method of asking the right questions, making rough guesses from past successes, setting checkpoints and letting the problem dictate the answer. It can be learned in the same way as grammar, and it has to be repeated for each new product and market because fixed rules go stale quickly. [L0330.103]
- The right approach is found through study and testing, and sometimes by accident. Expect to try many versions before one works, and keep learning from the results. [L1369.105]
- You do not need to be a creative genius to start. What you need is to recognize what already works and copy its model. [L1252.018]

### Research before writing

- Good advertising results from deep research. Expert writers often spend weeks reading and testing before writing a single ad. Hard work and preparation separate successful ads from guesses. [L1261.133]
- Motivation research helps the copywriter discover hidden desires, the taboos that limit how they can be expressed, the splits within a market, and early reactions to the wording of a headline. A research finding is a direction, not a headline. It tells you where not to go and the general area of the solution. [L0330.061]
- Before scripting any ad, the seller must know the product inside out, including its features, the benefits they create, and which goal or pain each benefit addresses. Without that product knowledge, no script should be written. [L1425.001]
- Before scripting, the seller must also understand the target audience's wants, needs, pain points, where they want to go and what they want to escape from. The ad must make the viewer feel the seller understands them and is on their side. [L1425.002]
- The method described works for problem-solving products, which is most products, where the buyer is trying to get from point A to point B, reach a goal or escape pain. [L1425.003]
- Customers arrive at an e-commerce store because they want to solve a problem, and that problem is the real reason they buy. Copy should start from the buyer's problem, not from the product's features. (Older advice, Source B 2021-22.) [L1157.001]
- Procedure for testing a concept before scaling:

1. Run a deep-dive research on Amazon reviews and Reddit threads for your niche and fill in the research form: pain points, exact customer language, USP, and ICP.
2. Write one clear main message per concept, built from what real users said.
3. Build one or two concepts with 3-5 creatives each. Use statics if you have not yet reached the proof-of-concept gate; use VSL once you hit $75-$100 per day.
4. Launch in a testing CBO, run it for 3 days, then read website purchases and ROAS.
5. Fold the winners into your main CBO.

[L1195.054]

- In e-commerce, a store does not need the best copy in the world to win. Beating most competitors' copy is enough to gain an edge, since many online stores have weak copy. (Older advice, Source B 2021-22.) [L1523.001]

### Planning an ad before writing

- Planning an ad can take weeks or months, and the result may be only five or ten words. Those words carry most of the ad's value. If they are right the ad can work, and if they are wrong nothing written after them will save it. Spend the time on the idea before writing body copy. [L0330.063]
- The unique idea for a product lies in the one element that makes its product and market different. Find that element through research into the market and study of the product, not by borrowing a formula. [L0330.064]
- Advertising is not about entertaining or pleasing the creator, but about the buyer's interests, so always write from the viewpoint of the typical buyer. Think of one real person who is likely to buy, not the mass audience. [L1261.137]
- Advertisers who sell to the buyer's interests, not the seller's, succeed far more often, because the seller's view tends to focus on the product rather than the buyer's needs. Imagine the buyer's face-to-face reaction to every line. [L1261.138]
- Give each letter one specific goal and make every sentence move the reader one step nearer to it. If a sentence does not support the goal, remove it. [L1369.104]
- Learn the rules as a checklist until they become second nature, then write from the feel of the message rather than from the checklist. Mechanical rules build the letter, but the idea behind it makes it work. [L1369.106]
- Procedure to plan a new ad before writing:

1. State the problem in plain words, for example that you want to sell this product.
2. Restate the problem in a different way, for example that you want to introduce the unique aspects of the product to the prospect.
3. Restate it again in a way that describes the buyer's experience, for example that the product should be easy to buy and seem fun.
4. Choose the restatement that feels closest to the ad you want to write.
5. List several big ideas or concepts for that restatement, then pick the one or two that make the most sense.
6. Visualize how the concept fits into the ad and check it against the restatement.
7. Stop and incubate.
8. Write the headline, then the subheadline, then the first sentence.
9. Block-diagram the finished ad to check its flow.

[L1366.037]

- Persuasion principles: Cialdini's six principles of persuasion are reciprocity, scarcity/urgency/FOMO, authority, social proof, commitment and consistency, and liking. Getting these right makes an ad strongly persuasive. [L1425.014]
- Reciprocity: giving value first, such as free ebooks in exchange for an email, free content, or value delivered up front, makes people more likely to buy when the offer arrives. [L1425.015]
- Authority: people trust experts and people with an air of power, so being featured in a magazine or endorsed by a doctor increases persuasion because the audience assumes they know what they are talking about. [L1425.016]
- Commitment and consistency: people want to stay consistent with their identity. Asking for small commitments, such as an email signup, followed by a small purchase and then a larger one, is easier than asking for a big sale up front, which can overwhelm people. [L1425.017]
- Liking is built from three parts: similarity, genuine compliments and cooperation. Similarity signals safety to the viewer, so the brand should show that it was founded by people like the audience. Compliments must be genuine, and cooperation means positioning the brand as a helper that moves the customer toward their goal, not as an obstacle. [L1425.018]
- Value proposition formula (Hormozi's value equation): Value = dream outcome × perceived likelihood of achievement, divided by time delay and effort and sacrifice. Increase the buyer's trust in reaching the outcome, then reduce time, effort and sacrifice, so the product seems quick, easy and costless. [L1425.019]

### Incubation: letting ideas form

- Problem solving through incubation:

1. Think intensely about the problem and gather all the relevant information, including the possible options.
2. Define the desired end result clearly.
3. Stop working on the problem and put off the decision until later, dismissing it from your conscious mind.
4. Before sleep, tell yourself you want the answer or idea by morning, and keep a notepad by the bed.
5. Write down any idea or solution as soon as you wake, before it fades.

[L1206.021]

- Creative solutions and insights often appear when the conscious mind is relaxed or engaged in something else, such as a routine activity or a walk. Continuing to struggle and worry during that time usually delays the solution. [L1206.022]
- Forcing a solution with strained effort tends to jam the automatic problem-solving process, which then produces anxiety instead of answers. Trust the process and act on the best available information instead. [L1206.023]
- Incubation procedure for copy:

1. Gather all material about the product and the customer.
2. Think through what you have read, and optionally jot down headlines, key points, the product's nature and the strongest reasons customers would buy.
3. Stop working on the project and do something pleasant and unrelated, without thinking about the copy.
4. When an idea appears, write it down at once.
5. Write a first draft from the material your mind has organized, without correcting spelling or grammar.

[L1366.038, L1366.113]

- Incubation runs in the background even when you do not work on the project. Thinking about the project again interrupts it and weakens the result. Give the project real breaks rather than forcing continuous effort. [L1366.039, L1366.114]
- Incubation speed and quality depend on time, creative orientation, environment and ego. Some pressure, such as deadlines or pride in the result, speeds up the process, but too little pressure slows it down. [L1366.040, L1366.115]
- Keep the creative team separate from routine operations. Copywriters need freedom to incubate, and their output drops when they are held to the same visible schedule as operational staff. [L1366.116]

### Lateral thinking

- Lateral thinking exercise for a stuck ad concept:

1. Pick a random source of words, such as a shuffled set of words or a dictionary.
2. Pull out the first three words you see.
3. Force a connection between those three words and the product you are selling.
4. Build an ad concept from the connection, keeping the selling goal in mind.

[L1366.042]

- When solving a marketing problem, step outside the original frame by relating it to something unrelated. A solution that looks impossible inside the problem often appears when the problem is connected to a different context. [L1366.044, L1366.118]

### Constraints and assumed limits

- Limitations force focused decisions. Setting a firm constraint on time, budget, materials or scope is a practical way to beat creative block. Waiting for unlimited resources usually stops the work entirely. [L1343.009]
- Tight limits make creative choices easier, such as one color for a product ad, a 60-second maximum for a video script, a fixed 100 USD test budget for a campaign, or a set vocabulary for a product description. Open-ended options often cause paralysis, while a hard boundary forces a decision. [L1343.044]
- Test assumed constraints instead of accepting them. Assumed constraints block good ideas. Common self-imposed limits include believing a product cannot sell at a high price or through mail, or that a certain audience will not respond. These limits should be tested rather than accepted. Disproved limits: a $190,000 limit on a used airplane was beaten by a $240,000 sale in 10 days, and a $600 product sold more than 3,000 units through mail order. [L1366.043, L1366.119]
- Experiment freely with low-stakes projects before you have public attention, because attention brings expectations that limit experimentation. Use the early period to test ideas without worrying about audience reactions or revenue pressure. [L1343.040]

### Practice, daily output and drafting

- Consistent small output compounds. Writing one page per day adds up to about 365 pages in a year, enough for a full novel. Measure progress by daily output over months, not by single big wins. [L1343.010]
- Practice produces the largest gains in copywriting. Write every day in small forms such as letters, postcards, articles and ads, because volume builds skill faster than study alone. [L1366.120]
- Accept that a first draft will be weak. Judge the draft by its emotional release, not by how it reads, because the real quality comes from editing. [L1366.121]
- Draft with the intuitive, emotional side of the mind, then edit with the analytical side. Ignore spelling and grammar while drafting, and switch to logic when editing. [L1366.122]
- In editing, refine copy so it expresses exactly the intended meaning with the fewest words. You may rearrange sentences, cut low-value words, substitute stronger words or add words for clarity, while keeping the same emotional feel. [L1366.123]
- Waiting to know who you are or what your brand stands for before you start producing keeps you stuck in planning. Identity and style usually emerge from doing the work regularly. [L1343.023]
- Base your creative output on subjects that genuinely interest you, not only on what you already know, because work drawn only from familiar experience tends to be dull. In e-commerce this means choosing products, angles and offers you would actually want to buy or use yourself. [L1343.034]
- Side projects and experiments often produce the best results, so keep several projects running and switch between them when one stalls. Returning to a paused project later is a productive way to deal with a lack of motivation. [L1343.026]
- When unsure what to do next, ask which option would make a better story for your work or career. This question helps you choose a direction that is interesting and worth pursuing. [L1343.032]
- Staying relevant after a viral win is the hardest part. You need to produce many new video ideas every day to keep views steady. [L1429.024]

### Idea capture

- Idea capture method (older advice, Source B 2021-22):

1. When a new idea appears during work, write it down in the Notes column or as a card without leaving the current tracking interface.
2. Return to the current task right away.
3. Review the Notes panels regularly so recorded ideas are not lost.

[L1364.045]

- Writing down an idea removes it from working memory, so focus can return to the current task without losing the idea. Ideas that are not recorded are often forgotten by the end of the day. (Older advice, Source B 2021-22.) [L1364.046]
- Feeling stuck or blank when brainstorming is normal and does not mean you lack the ability to find a market. Ideas often arrive later when you are not forcing them, so keep the list open and add ideas as they come. (Older advice, Source B 2021-22.) [L0996.015]

### Influences and reference collecting

- Almost every new idea is a combination of earlier ideas, so original work comes from recombining existing influences rather than starting from nothing. Treating influence as normal, not as a weakness, frees you to build on what already works. [L1343.001]
- The quality of your reference inputs caps the quality of your output, so collect selectively and only keep sources that genuinely appeal to you. Indiscriminate collecting dilutes your taste and makes your work average. [L1343.002]
- Influence tree procedure:

1. Pick one creator or brand you admire and study everything about their work.
2. Identify three people or brands that this creator was influenced by and study them the same way.
3. Repeat this chain as far back as you can go, building a tree of influences.
4. Start your own work as a new branch that grows from that tree.

[L1343.004]

- Copy-and-amplify procedure:

1. Copy the work of the creators you admire closely enough to understand how it is built.
2. Note exactly where your version falls short of the original.
3. Amplify those differences, since they show what you add that is unique.
4. Develop that difference into your own product, ad or content.

[L1343.005]

- Experience and broad knowledge give the copywriter more parts to combine into new ideas. Curiosity, varied hobbies (interests), travel and learning new skills all add material that later becomes an unexpected angle. [L1366.041, L1366.117]
- Once you internalize the no-brand-loyalty principle, product opportunities become visible everywhere: in shopping malls, home interiors, magazines, and television. This accelerates your ability to identify viable dropshipping niches. [L0319.017]
- Copying what already works is a legitimate starting point. Recognize the model behind a successful product, ad or store and adapt it, then build your difference on top of it. [L1252.018]

### Working environment: analog and digital

- Computers are strong for editing and publishing but weak for generating ideas, because deleting and perfecting starts too early. Generate ideas by hand or in a sketchbook first, and bring in the computer only once the thinking is mostly done. [L1343.015]
- Setting up two separate workstations:

1. Set up two separate workstations: an analog station with no electronics and a digital station with your computer, scanner and other devices.
2. Stock the analog station with paper, pens, markers, sticky notes and index cards, which can be bought for about $10.
3. Generate and sketch ideas at the analog station, standing up if possible, and pin ideas on walls to find patterns.
4. Move to the digital station to execute, edit and publish the ideas.
5. When you lose momentum, return to the analog station and keep exploring.

[L1343.014]

### Walks, breaks and stepping away

- When stuck on a problem, leave the computer and take a walk, go to the gym, or do a routine task, because solutions often come when you are not actively searching. Searching online is still useful, but do not replace stepping away with constant thinking about the issue. [L1128.018]
- Schedule unstructured time with no goal, since boredom and routine tasks such as washing dishes or walking often trigger new ideas. Keeping a daily routine matters more than finding large blocks of free time. [L1343.027]

## Plan promotions, seasons and content calendars

### Monthly Promotion Calendar

#### Building the 12-month calendar

1. Write the next 12 months in a spreadsheet or on paper, starting with the current month. [L1204.043]
2. Assign one offer per month from your arsenal and rotate through the list. [L1204.043]
3. In the notes column, write a short hook tied to a holiday, season or event. [L1204.043]
4. Define eligibility for each offer based on profit, such as a minimum order value or a specific product purchase. [L1204.043]
5. Create the emails and ads months in advance. [L1204.043]
6. Schedule the offers in Klaviyo, Google and Facebook. [L1204.043]

#### Sample calendar

| Month | Offer | Hook or reason |
|---|---|---|
| June | Free summer wheat recipe pack, eligible with any starter kit | Be ready for the 4th of July barbecue |
| August | Entry into a drawing for a trip to a homebrewing conference | Oktoberfest happens in September and October |
| September | 10 percent off | Labor Day sale |
| October | Free white glove delivery | Seasonal delivery offer |
| November | Bonus gift on orders over 1,000 dollars | Black Friday and Cyber Monday |
| December | Free bonus gift with all orders | Holiday shopping |
| January | 10 percent off again | Repeated offer with a new reason |

[L1204.044]

#### Offer windows and timing

- Run each monthly offer in a fixed window of about 3 to 5 days within the month, not for the whole month. [L1204.045]
- Use shorter windows for limited quantities or clearance stock to create urgency. Use longer windows for large purchases that need time to decide. [L1204.045]
- End offers on a weekday, such as a Friday. Open and click rates were highest on weekdays, possibly because people read email and social media during working hours. [L1204.047]
- Monthly campaigns on the Display Network run 4 to 5 days per month, for example October 29 to 31 for a Halloween sale. Use static ads instead of dynamic ads, and target a broader 180-day website visitor audience. [L0210.012]
- Use an evergreen URL such as /promotions rather than a time-specific URL such as /halloween-sale. The same link can then be reused across all monthly campaigns, and Google can build search authority on that page. [L0210.013]

#### Running and improving the programme

- A monthly promotion programme often needs several months before it reliably adds 20 to 30 percent of monthly revenue. A strong result can come in the first month, but expect three to six months of testing different offers before the programme is stable. [L1204.067]
- Do not rotate a weak offer back into the plan later. Replace it with a different offer based on the results, and keep repeating the offers that consistently win. [L1204.058]
- Even very small remarketing audiences are worth emailing for each promotion, because each small sale from existing contacts costs almost nothing. [L1115.078]
- Treat failed promotions as data, not waste. Early failures show which directions do not work. Reviewing each one for what did and did not work makes the next promotion more likely to sell. [L1365.054]

### Preparing Promotions Ahead of Time

- Creating emails and ads in the week of the sale is a mistake. Spend one to two hours a week on promotion assets and schedule everything in advance in Klaviyo, Google and Facebook. [L1204.056]
- Start planning Q4 promotions about eight weeks before the first promotion, rather than a week before Halloween, to leave time for building offers, emails and ads. [L1285.098]
- Prepare the emails, ad copy and images for every Q4 promotion ahead of time, ideally by mid-October. Last-minute creation produces rushed assets and missed launch dates. [L1287.006]
- Waiting until the week before a holiday to plan the promotion leaves no time for offers, emails and ads. Set aside planning time several weeks ahead and work through the offers, emails and questions early. [L1223.030]
- Quarterly planning across five promotions is easier when the team starts from historical best-performing copy and offers. Proven subject lines and headlines reduce the planning load and improve the chance of success. [L1223.031]

### Seasonal Lead Times

- Seasonal products should be tested early. For example, Halloween items tested in early September sell significantly better than when competitors start testing. Starting 6 to 8 weeks before an event gives first-mover advantage and tests content performance in lower-competition inventory. [L0309.014]
- Seasonal events such as Valentine's Day need preparation about one and a half to two months ahead. The store should be built and pre-ads running to pre-target the event. [L0561.044]
- Valentine's Day is the next major sales window after the current period. Plan inventory, campaigns and bundles accordingly. [L0324.040]
- Plan a product launch before the peak season, such as Q4. Prepare winning products in advance so they can be rolled out in about three days once demand arrives. [L1444.015]

### Q4 Preparation

- Start preparing Q4 promotions in September so the store, suppliers and promotion assets are ready about two months ahead, ideally by Halloween. Preparing in mid-October leaves no time to fix problems. [L1224.012]
- Have the store, suppliers and promotion assets ready by about Halloween. That leaves roughly two months before the peak. Sales usually start climbing in late October and November, so earlier preparation is better. [L1287.005]
- Sources differ on the launch target. A 2023 source gives mid-November as the time to finish supplier approvals and store launch for holiday traffic. [L1114.063] The 2024 and 2025 sources ask for readiness by Halloween, with planning starting about eight weeks before the first promotion. [L1285.098, L1287.005] Plan to the Halloween target. The mid-November date is the later option named in the 2023 source. [L1114.063]
- Q4 pressure is often misplaced. One seller's first success came in Q1, not Q4, so do not panic about the holiday window. [L0820.041]

### Black Friday and Cyber Monday

- Treat Black Friday as a deadline to launch by, not as the finish line. A store launched by then with a few suppliers, some traffic and some sales is on track. Plan the business as a long-term venture. [L1097.043]
- Prepare promotions around Black Friday and Cyber Monday in advance, and have stores ready before the holiday rush. [L1114.063]

### Supplier Timing Around Holidays

- Plan supplier outreach around the holidays. Late December and the days around Christmas through New Year are poor for approvals. Set up the store before the holidays and restart outreach about one week into January. (Older advice, 2021.) [L0469.110]
- When suppliers go quiet during Black Friday and Cyber Monday, it usually reflects their busiest weeks, not a permanent refusal. Circle back in the following weeks, especially in the Q5 window. (Older advice, 2020.) [L0468.180]

### Content Calendar and Posting Consistency

#### Why a calendar helps

- A content calendar helps creators produce ahead of time, protects against missed upload targets and keeps posting consistent when motivation drops. [L0795.003]
- A content calendar should allow setting dates, times, a clear objective, goals and deadlines. Short bullet notes on what each video covers and why it is posted should sit alongside these. [L0795.005]
- The phone's built-in reminders app, or the Android equivalent, can send an alert before each scheduled upload. [L0795.005]

#### Minimum requirements before building a calendar

1. Choose the product you want to test.
2. Decide how you will create the videos.
3. Create a TikTok account.
4. Film videos on a phone.
5. Edit the footage in Splice.
6. Draft and finish the video in TikTok, adding text-to-speech, text and small edits.
7. Decide the upload times and the schedule that suits your audience.

[L0795.004]

#### Launch and continuity

- A finished store and a chosen product give no advantage if no videos are posted. A competitor who started posting later can overtake a seller who built the store earlier. Launch with a posting schedule, not only with a finished store. [L0657.051]
- Keep uploading consistently even when early videos underperform, because a viral result can arrive weeks later. One account went viral about two weeks after its first viral attempt. [L0764.042]
- Keep posting at a steady pace even when motivation and energy are low. The referenced creator uploaded 3 videos per day during discouraging periods, and that consistency led to the breakthrough. Consistency matters more than perfect planning. [L0821.012]
- Results from a short-video channel should be judged after about three months of consistent posting, not after one or two weeks. [L1131.055]
- Plan for roughly 2 to 4 hours of work per day on this Instagram viral process. [L1367.015]

#### Systems over motivation

- Motivation fades, so rely on systems: reusable video formats, templates, batch production and a repeatable process. Systems keep output steady when inspiration is low. [L1457.016]
- Consistent posting feeds the algorithm. Good content without a posting strategy is often shown to the wrong people. Consistency and planning are weighted above the quality of a single video. [L1457.005]

#### Quantity versus quality

- For short-video posting, consistency and planning are weighted above the quality of a single video. [L1457.005, L0821.012]
- For content marketing with articles, it is better to publish fewer pieces of very high quality than many shallow ones. (Older advice, 2021-2022.) [L1461.002]
- Both apply to different channels. Keep a steady cadence on short-video channels, and keep each long-form piece deep and high quality. [L1457.005, L0821.012, L1461.002]

#### Product fit

- Many accounts that go viral do not sell because the content is not built around the product. Decide early whether the product fits your content plan and your skills. [L1444.016]

### Content Marketing: Long-Horizon Commitment

#### Patience and time

- Commit to a content marketing strategy for at least three months before judging it, and publish consistently during that period. One article does nothing. Results come from a steady stream of articles over several months, the same as with a blog. [L0380.004]
- Content marketing takes months to build traffic and sales, so judge it over a long horizon instead of by quick returns. Keep producing and refining the system while waiting for the compounding results. [L0382.060]
- Before committing, weigh the strategy against your available time, because content marketing is demanding to run alone. If the workload feels overwhelming, keep to the seven-step checklist and outsource one task at a time, starting with writing. [L0380.032]
- Brand building is not a one-time task. A single meme or post will not create a community. The content has to keep going, with new material added regularly over the long term. [L0487.066]

#### Compounding and simplicity (older advice, 2021-2022)

- Content and SEO work compound like a snowball. It starts slowly and gains momentum over time. Starting early gets the compounding going sooner, even though early results are small. [L0383.003]
- Building the full marketing system in an existing online store can take up to 12 months, depending on how much time the owner can commit. It is a long, steady build rather than a quick fix. [L0414.053]
- A content marketing plan should stay deliberately simple. Choose the format, plan the first 12 ideas, assign who produces the content, publish a steady number of pieces each month and promote them. Adding complexity early is usually unnecessary. [L0383.015]
- A pre-set content plan removes the daily decision of what to publish next, which is where content work usually stalls. Ideas are already chosen, so the producer can start work without re-planning. [L0384.002]
- Once a piece is locked into the plan with its promotion steps, carry those steps out as written. Change them only for a strong reason, because the value of planning is not having to decide again later. [L0384.015]
- Do not assume that putting a product on your website will bring visitors by itself. Have a written channel plan covering how customers will find the product from the start. [L0768.022]

### Email and Follow-Up

- Follow-up marketing is best built gradually. Start with email sequences for best-selling products, and expand the number of flows and retargeting audiences over time. (Older advice, 2021-2022.) [L0493.022]
- Once the loyalty program and its automated emails are set up, they run with little ongoing work. Expect to revisit the setup occasionally, but the main effort is at the start. (Older advice, 2021-2022.) [L0956.014]
- Post-purchase offers are worth testing even when most buyers decline. A small share of customers who said no to several offers can still convert on the final one, so this revenue is worth pursuing. [L1413.002]
- The time between a first visit and a purchase can range from days to weeks or even months, especially for higher-ticket products. Follow-up has to bridge that gap, because many competitors compete for the same customer's attention at any moment. (Older advice, 2021-2022.) [L1497.002]
- Holding a shopper's attention is what gives you the chance to get their money. Judge follow-up by whether it keeps the market engaged on the way to a purchase, not only by transaction count or conversion rate. (Older advice, 2021-2022.) [L1497.003]
- Full implementation of follow-up takes time, because emails must be written and put in place. Optimising follow-up is an ongoing process over months and even years, not something finished at once. (Older advice, 2021-2022.) [L1497.007]

### Attention and Opening Seconds

- Attention is increasingly fragmented because of growing social media use, full inboxes and far more websites and advertisers than a decade or fifteen years ago. Brands must grab attention quickly and then hold it, since people switch between things rapidly. (Older advice, 2021-2022.) [L1117.004]
- Average human attention span was about 9 to 10 seconds in 2020, compared with minutes measured 15 to 20 years earlier. Use this as a reason to make the first seconds of any ad, page or content count, not as a precise benchmark. (Older advice, 2021-2022.) [L1117.005]

### Referral and Thank-You Scheduling

#### Why referral and thanks come first

- Referral marketing turns past customers into a traffic source, because they recommend the store to family and friends who then buy. Plan a referral funnel as part of the traffic mix. [L0597.013]
- Thanking customers is the first step of a referral sequence because it is simple and rarely done by online stores. Expressing gratitude also supports the owner's own wellbeing and lowers stress, which helps balance the frequent setbacks of running an online business. (Older advice, 2021-2022.) [L0406.005]

#### Scheduling thank-you messages

- Thank-you messages must be scheduled as recurring tasks in a daily to-do list, a calendar or a team member's list. If they are not, they pile up and get postponed until the backlog becomes too large to handle. (Older advice, 2021-2022.) [L0406.028]

Procedure (older advice, 2021-2022):

1. Choose the thank-you method: video, handwritten note, or both.
2. Set up the matching tool integration with Shopify.
3. Add the thank-you task to your own daily to-do list or to a team member's daily to-do list.
4. If you do not do the task yourself, write it into the SOP of the team member most relevant to it, such as the order processor or phone customer service.

[L0406.029]

### Dream 100 Time Blocks

#### Principles (older advice, 2021-2022)

- The Dream 100 process does not produce results overnight, with rare exceptions. It requires consistent effort over a long period, and the payoff comes later. [L0412.008]
- Dream 100 relationship building belongs in the important but not urgent category of a prioritisation matrix. Schedule it as a recurring task rather than treating it as something that demands immediate attention. [L0529.001]
- Consistency matters more than intensity for Dream 100 work. A small daily time block beats occasional long sessions. The goal is a sustainable routine that keeps running over months. [L0529.004]
- Dream 100 work compounds over time, similar to SEO, so the earlier you start, the earlier you see results. Begin conservatively with small amounts of work and build up gradually over the coming months. [L0529.011]

#### Time budget

| Workday length | Dream 100 time per day |
|---|---|
| Standard 6 to 8 hours | About 30 minutes |
| Shorter, about 4 hours | About 15 minutes |

[L0529.005, L0529.006]

- Spending two to three hours per day on Dream 100 activities is too much. That time is taken from tasks only the owner can do, so keep the daily amount small. [L0529.010]

#### Scheduling the block

1. Add Dream 100 time to your daily or weekly planning, as a block you can slot into your schedule.
2. Decide whether to do it at the start or the end of the workday, based on your own schedule.
3. Use the end of the day if your higher-level thinking is already used up, since the work mainly involves reading, asking questions and leaving short comments.
4. Keep the block short and move on to other tasks afterwards.

[L0529.009]

#### Further details

- Pinterest is a learning environment: a product that fails may still work once the creative or offer changes, so test several products before deciding. In the lesson's experience roughly one product in five or six became a winner. [L1135.048]

## Other details

### Planning and review routine

**Business fundamentals action plan (older advice, 2021-2022):** Work through these steps in this order.
1. Write a vision document describing the business's 10-year vision. [L0441.001]
2. Based on the 10-year vision, set 3-year and 1-year goals and record them in the tracking document. [L0441.001]
3. Identify the first set of Rocks for the next 90 days, based on the goals. [L0441.001]
4. Build weekly and daily to-do lists from the Rocks, with the most important Rock task at the top of each list. [L0441.001]
5. Add to the calendar a fixed daily time for reviewing the to-do list, and a weekly time for planning the following week's list. [L0441.001]
6. Schedule quarterly review times to set new Rocks and an annual review time to assess the business against its goals and set goals for the next year. [L0441.001]
7. Complete the customer worksheet (what you know about your customer and what you still need to find out). [L0441.001]
8. Complete the competitor analysis worksheet using data from competitors' websites and traffic tools. [L0441.001]
9. Review the financial tracking system and replace it with a proven system if it does not work. [L0441.001]
10. Calculate the target cost per sale with the traffic calculator, record the numbers, and compare them with the financial tracking to monitor profit day to day. [L0441.001]

- Keep the action checklist as a printable document and tick off each item once it is done. This makes it easy to confirm that no planning step (vision, goals, Rocks, worksheets, calendar times) has been skipped. (older advice, 2021-2022) [L0441.015]
- Complete the customer worksheet before deciding on positioning and offers. It records what you already know about your target customer and which customer facts you still need to find out. (older advice, 2021-2022) [L0441.008]
- Three factors decide whether a dropshipper succeeds, because knowledge alone is not enough: [L0519.002]
  - A clear vision of the future you.
  - Discipline: knowing when and how to do each task.
  - Organization: structures, delegation and clear next steps.

### Customer and offer definition

- Identify your dream customer before choosing products and audiences. Dotcom Secrets by Russell Brunson is recommended for this. [L0519.011]
- Read the Alex Hormozi books for offer ideas, and the first part of Dotcom Secrets to identify the dream customer and dream offer. [L0650.053]
- Do not sell a product on a topic you cannot teach. Learn a niche properly first, because real expertise lets you judge and improve competitor offers. [L0513.040]
- Build credibility by showing who you are, your experience and why the buyer should trust you, such as your profession or years of practice in the niche. [L0513.039]

### Brand building and store quality

- Build your brand gradually through iterative improvement over years rather than attempting overnight changes. This enables sustainable growth and continuous brand evolution. [L0260.025]
- The Oodie brand was built iteratively over 4 to 5 years and continues to be refined. Sustainable brand building requires long-term commitment and ongoing iteration. [L0260.026]
- First impressions determine customer lifetime value. Treat every interaction (shipping, support, unboxing) as brand-building, not as a one-time transaction. [L0324.039]
- Good intentions amplify skill but cannot replace it. Buyers sense whether a seller is trying to help or to extract money, and that difference affects long-term results. [L0378.111]
- The purpose of a design is to make the message understood and to lead the viewer toward the action the business wants. If too many elements compete for attention, none of them will be seen first. [L0755.031]
- If a self-built store does not look close to competitor sites, get help rather than continuing with a poor result. Comparing your site with competitor sites is a quick check for quality. [L0921.014]
- Ask the supplier before using their reviews on your store, since reviews taken without permission can cause trouble with Google. Only use them after the supplier explicitly agrees. (older advice, 2021) [L0853.100]
- Use an expert reviewer for scientific or technical claims before publication, because a writer may draw wrong conclusions from facts. Check any product-specific claim with someone who knows the science. [L1261.135]

### Legal, banking and tax setup (older advice, 2021-2022)

**Legal and banking action steps, in order:**
1. Decide the country you will sell in: your own country if it has an e-commerce market, otherwise the US. [L1405.035]
2. Form the business entity: in the US an LLC or S-Corp; in Australia usually a sole trader, or a Pty Ltd if an accountant advises it. [L1405.035]
3. Obtain the tax ID for your country: in the US an EIN and, if your state has sales tax, a sales tax permit; in Australia at least an ABN. [L1405.035]
4. Find a business accountant if you do not already have one, preferably one who knows dropshipping. [L1405.035]
5. Open a business bank account with low or no fees (usually free to open) and a business credit card with rewards, or a debit card on the Visa or Mastercard network if no credit card is possible. [L1405.035]

### Supplier relationships

- Present your business to suppliers as a long-term operation, not a quick-money project, with customer service as its main priority. [L1422.020]
- Income from a store is not a reason to stop supplier approvals or product uploads. Growth needs a constant flow of new products and suppliers, not one push. [L0359.108]
- When a supplier issue occurs, evaluate the total relationship (2022, older advice):
  - If the supplier ships correctly most of the time and is otherwise responsive, work through isolated problems.
  - If a pattern of poor behavior emerges, cut them off. [L0249.089]
- Never misrepresent your business. Do not claim years of experience, past sales or a track record you do not have. Lying to suppliers tends to become a habit that spreads to customers and staff and hurts the business in the long run. Being a new business is no disadvantage in itself and does not automatically lead to a no. (older advice, 2021-2022) [L0929.006]
- A supplier refusal for now does not mean failure. If a supplier says they are not taking new retailers, record the reason and the date they suggested, then follow up at that time. [L0928.013]
- A no from a supplier usually means no for now, not no forever. Stay open-minded, energetic and confident. Suppliers are businesses that want to make money and want customers who respect their brand. [L0933.032]
- When a supplier says no or gives vague reasons, follow up on a set schedule rather than giving up. Repeated, polite contact with a status update shows commitment and does not get you blacklisted. (2023) [L0835.130]
- Confidence on supplier calls comes from knowing your subject well. Do not fake knowledge; study the material until you can speak about the business with real understanding. [L0927.005]
- When a supplier asks about annual sales goals for a brand-new store, a reasonable answer is a figure in a range between 2 and 500,000. The currency was not specified. (2023) [L0934.190]

**Handling a no from a supplier, in order:** [L0928.014]
1. Thank them and make sure you have said everything you can to get approved. [L0928.014]
2. Note in the worksheet exactly what they said, including any date they suggested to call back. [L0928.014]
3. Set a reminder on your phone or in Google Calendar for that date. [L0928.014]
4. Follow up when the reminder comes. [L0928.014]

### Competitors and market awareness

- Study competitors as future rivals, but do not take their work. Success in dropshipping should come from your own effort and original work. [L0365.003]
- Viewing Google Ads updates as opportunities to optimize ahead of competitors gives a competitive advantage. Most businesses respond slowly to platform changes. [L0017.003]

### Sales conversations (High Probability approach)

- Traditional selling aims to get the buyer to act, so every tactic becomes a form of pressure that triggers resistance. The High Probability approach replaces that goal with one question: is there a mutually acceptable basis for doing business? If not, both sides end the process early without a deal. [L0770.001]
- Set aside old sales beliefs before judging a new method, because habits built on the old goal can conflict with the new one. Discomfort is normal while you practice and fades with repetition, so treat it as the cost of learning rather than proof that the method is wrong. [L0770.002]
- Random negative reinforcement means a few sales won through ineffective tactics can strengthen habits that fail over time. Judge your methods by results across many calls, not by individual wins. [L0770.003]
- Never try to convince a prospect that he needs or wants your offer. If he clearly understands what you offer and it does not match what he wants, end the visit. If he really wants it, he will not let you go. [L0770.004]
- Avoid the five-step selling sequence of attention, interest, desire, conviction and action as a selling model. It is built on manipulation, wastes time on people who will not buy, and places the close at the end when it should be the whole process. [L0770.005]
- When several prospects that sounded qualified all end without a commitment, check three causes:
  - You are still new to the method.
  - You read weak signals as strong ones.
  - You slipped into answering or selling.
  
  Each cause can be corrected, and only the last one is about your behavior on the call. [L0770.080]

### Affiliate and lead value

- Affiliate marketing done only for quick money tends to fail. Promoting products you have not used, or do not believe in, shows in your emails and makes your recommendations less convincing. [L0514.034]
- Promote only products you have used, know someone who has used successfully, or have investigated thoroughly with a clear view of pros and cons. Recommending something you believe in is easier and more credible than recommending something you have not tested. [L0514.035]
- Focus on giving value to the audience instead of trying to extract money from them. A sincere recommendation is more likely to produce long-term sales than a short-term push. [L0514.036]
- The five lead value techniques are:
  - Cross promoting your own related stores.
  - Cross promoting affiliate stores.
  - Promoting information products.
  - Upselling add-ons at checkout.
  - Upselling intangible services.
  
  All of them depend on first collecting emails and installing tracking pixels on visitors. [L0923.005]
- Affiliate program setting (older advice, 2021-2022): members recommend the program with their personal affiliate link, found under the resources section of the lesson page. Each person who joins through the link earns the referrer 500 USD, and four to five referrals can recover the cost of the course. Referred members' later spending can also pay out a share of their purchases. [L1498.020]

### Platform and ad settings

**Display campaign for dynamic remarketing (setting):** [L0210.010]
1. Check Dynamic ads for personalized ads. [L0210.010]
2. Select the Google Merchant Center data feed. [L0210.010]
3. Set focus to viewable impressions. [L0210.010]
4. Use no filters to show all products. [L0210.010]
5. Set the CPM bid to $10. [L0210.010]
6. Target the smallest recent website visitor audience available (7 days, 30 days, or larger if traffic is low). [L0210.010]
7. Turn off optimized targeting. [L0210.010]
8. Turn off audience expansion. [L0210.010]

- Do not include video extensions in display campaigns. Keep dynamic product display ads strictly as images with product links, not video. [L0210.011]
- Do not start campaigns immediately after creating the TikTok Ads account. Wait until the account review finishes (up to 24 hours) and the status shows as Active. [L1373.006]
- On Instagram, a video uploaded as a Reel also appears as a regular feed post, so one upload covers both formats. Verify this in the upload screen when you create the Reel. [L1186.040]
- Low reach is often caused by strikes, restrictions or reports rather than poor content. Review account notices and guidelines before concluding that content is the problem. [L1384.047]
- Bing Webmaster Tools shows a profile completion percentage, which was 20% right after import. Filling in personal, professional or company details is optional, and it is unclear whether it affects performance, so it should not be treated as a required setup step. [L0303.013]

### Learning, skills and mindset

- Knowledge alone does not make a dropshipper successful. See the three factors under Planning and review routine. [L0519.002]
- Habits that help owners grow a large business are the same for very large and very small companies. Developing yourself into a person capable of growing a large business applies regardless of current size. (older advice, 2021-2022) [L1256.002]
- Customer service skills improve with practice, so expect to become faster and more comfortable over the first months. Learning by doing is the main way to build this skill. (older advice, 2021-2022) [L0909.025]
- Mind OS, a book on the psychology of the self, was recommended as one of the few useful books for building inner confidence. The price quoted was roughly $200 to $500, so it is an expensive purchase. [L0701.040]
- Coding requires a real interest in the work, because progress is slow and frustrating on many days. Staying committed through setbacks is part of building the skill. [L0709.039]

### Source notes

- Source A members-only podcast episode (no date given): it only announces updates on an event (a retreat in 2025, a Black Friday mention) and promises to answer community questions in a Discord group. It has no actionable rules, numbers, procedures or settings, so nothing usable can be extracted. [L0627.001]

#### Further details

Steps to create a personal copy of a shared self-assessment document in Google Docs:
1. Open the shared document link.
2. Click File in the top left corner.
3. Click Make a copy.
4. Answer every question in your own copy, not in the shared original.
[L1503.003]
